# Ms-116

### <span class="fac">[IIr\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/IIr.webp)</span>

## Philosophical Remarks XII

### <span class="fac">[1\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/1.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Can one _understand_ anything other than a sentence? Or: Is it not a sentence until one understands it? Thus: Can one understand anything other than _a sentence_?

### <span class="fac">[1\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/1.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> One might like to talk about “experiencing a sentence.” Can this experience be written down? –

### <span class="fac">[1\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/1.webp),[2\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/2.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Consider: in a certain sense, there is no half-sentence. That is to say, what applies to the half-sentence also applies to the word, namely that it only has sense in the context of the sentence. (A horse-leap consists of a straight and an oblique movement. Now compare: “a half horse-leap,” & “a half bread roll.” A half bread roll is better than no bread roll at all. –)

### <span class="fac">[2\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/2.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Consider: ‘Understanding only begins with the sentence. (And that is why it does not interest us.)’

### <span class="fac">[2\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/2.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2 = 3˙2</span> A temptation to believe that the word “understand,” the expression “to understand a sentence,” are _metalogical_ words.

### <span class="fac">[2\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/2.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Why should understanding concern us? We must understand the sign in that it is a sign for us!

### <span class="fac">[2\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/2.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> ‘It would also be strange if science, mathematics, used sentences, but did not speak of their understanding.’

### <span class="fac">[2\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/2.webp),[3\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/3.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">5</span> One sees the essential thing in understanding, the inessential thing in the sign. – By the way, what is the point of the sign at all? – Only to make oneself understandable to others? But how is that possible? I mean, – how does this happen? I mean, – how does this happen? – Here the sign is regarded as a medicine that should produce the same state in the other person that I have.

### <span class="fac">[3\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/3.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> In answer to the question “what do you mean?” we reply: “I mean this & that,” (&) not: “I mean what I mean by the sentence ‥‥‥.”

### <span class="fac">[3\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/3.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2 = 2˙2</span> A temptation to believe that the meaning of the word “understand,” the expression “to understand a sentence,” is _metalogical_. “Understand” & “mean” are words like all others.

### <span class="fac">[3\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/3.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> What a sentence _means_ is expressed in an explanation. Thus, in the end, opinion falls completely out of the language, and therefore out of consideration; & there remains the language, the only thing we can consider.

### <span class="fac">[4\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/4.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> ‘Language must speak for itself.’

### <span class="fac">[4\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/4.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> ‘All I can do in language is say _something_: say _the one_. (Say the one within the space of what I could have said.)’

### <span class="fac">[4\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/4.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> ‘Spoken language is explained by language. Therefore, one cannot explain _the language_ in this sense.’

### <span class="fac">[4\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/4.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> An interpretation is always only _one_ in contrast to _another_. It attaches itself to the sign & incorporates it into a (further) system.

### <span class="fac">[4\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/4.webp),[5\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/5.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">5</span> When Frege speaks against the formal conception of arithmetic, he says, as it were, that these petty explanations concerning the symbols are superfluous if we understand them. And understanding is, as it were, seeing a picture – from which all the rules then follow (thereby becoming understandable). But Frege, I believe, did not see that this picture is only another sign, or even a calculus, which explains the written calculus to us. And what we call understanding a language is, in general, like understanding a calculus, which comes from the fact that we learn the reasons for its origin or its application. And there too, we learn a readily comprehensible symbolism instead of the foreign one. (Understanding here means something similar to being able to overlook it.)

### <span class="fac">[5\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/5.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> If complicated mental processes must take place behind the facade of the symbols when understanding the word “and” (e.g.) – how is it that they are never mentioned in logic, nor need be?

### <span class="fac">[5\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/5.webp),[6\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/6.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> When I give someone a command, it is _quite enough_ for me to give them a sign. And I would never say: These are just words, & I must look beyond the words. Likewise, when I have asked someone something & they give me an answer (i.e., a sign), I am satisfied—that was my expectation—& do not object: That is merely an answer.

### <span class="fac">[6\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/6.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> But if one says: “how am I supposed to know what he means, I only see his signs,” I say: “how is _he_ supposed to know what he means, he only has his signs too.”

### <span class="fac">[6\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/6.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “But I did mean something when I said that!” – Well, but how can we find out what it is? Only, presumably, by him telling us. If we are not to take his other behaviour as a criterion, then (it) is what he explains to us.

### <span class="fac">[6\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/6.webp),[7\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/7.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Imagine that someone had originally learned chess as a writing game and knew nothing of the _interpretation_ of this game as a board game. Later, it would be shown to him.

### <span class="fac">[7\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/7.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “You made a movement with your hand; did you mean something by it? – I thought you meant that I should come to you.” So he could mean something, or also mean nothing. And if the former: then, what he also expressed, or something else? Did he mean something other than this with his expression, or did he only _mean_ his expression?

### <span class="fac">[7\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/7.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Could one also answer: “I meant something with this movement, which I can only express through this movement”?

### <span class="fac">[7\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/7.webp),[8\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/8.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> ‘We distinguish language from what is not language; writing from what is not writing. We see lines, on a wall for example, & say we understand them; & others – & say, they mean nothing (or, to _us_ nothing). This characterizes a very general experience, which we could call: “understanding something as language” – quite apart from what we read out of the lines (etc.).’

### <span class="fac">[8\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/8.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> I see a German inscription & a Chinese one: is the Chinese one perhaps unsuitable for communicating something? ‒ ‒ I say: I have not learned Chinese. But this only indicates the probable cause of the present phenomenon, which alone interests us now.

### <span class="fac">[8\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/8.webp),[9\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/9.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Do we arbitrarily assign interpretations to the words that are said to us? Does not the experience of understanding come with the experience of hearing (or seeing) the signs, when we understand the language? When someone says something to me & I understand (it), this happens to me in the same way as when I hear. And here understanding is the phenomenon – the phenomena, which occur when I hear a German sentence, & which distinguish this hearing from hearing a sentence in a language unknown to me.

### <span class="fac">[9\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/9.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Let us think of a cipher: a sentence is given to me in the cipher & also its key; then I am, of course, in one respect given everything for understanding the sentence. And yet I would answer the question “Do you understand this sentence?”, No, not yet; I must first decipher it. And only when I have, for example, translated it into German, would I say: “now I understand it.” If one asks oneself here: “At what point in this translation do I (first) understand the sentence?”, one gains an insight into the essence of what we call “understanding.”

### <span class="fac">[10\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/10.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> I say a sentence: “I see a black circle”; but these _particular_ words are not what matters – so let us substitute these with: “a b c d e.” But now, when I read that, I cannot immediately connect it with the sense mentioned above. – I am not used – one might say – to saying “a” instead of “I” & “b” instead of “see” & “c” instead of “a,” etc. But I do not mean that I am not used to associating “a” immediately with the word “I,” but rather I am not used to using “a” in place of “I” – in the sense of “I.”

### <span class="fac">[10\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/10.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “I don’t just say that, I also mean something by it.” – If one considers what happens within us when we _mean_ words (and not just say them), it seems as if something is then coupled with these words, whereas otherwise they run empty. – As if they, as it were, intervened in us.

### <span class="fac">[11\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/11.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> ‘I understand an order as an order, i.e., I don’t just see in it this structure of sounds or strokes, but it has – in a way – an influence on me. I react to the order (even before I obey it) differently than, for example, to a communication, or a question. (I read it in a different tone, with a different mien & gesture.)’

### <span class="fac">[11\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/11.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The sentence, when I understand it, gains depth for me.

### <span class="fac">[11\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/11.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> In a story, the sentence says: “After he had said that, he left her, as on the previous day.” – If one asks me whether I understand this sentence, it is not entirely easy to answer. It is a German sentence, & in that respect I understand it. I would know how one could use this sentence; I could even invent a context for it. And yet I do not understand it _in the way_ that I would understand it if I had read the story up to this sentence.

### <span class="fac">[12\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/12.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What does it mean to understand a picture, a drawing? There, too, there is understanding & not understanding. And also there, these expressions can mean different things. The picture is supposed to represent an arrangement of objects – for example, a still life; but I do not understand one part of the picture: i.e., I am not able to see bodies there, but only see patches of color on the surface of the picture. – Or I see everything physically, but the picture depicts objects that I have (never) seen before. And then there is the case where something is obviously (e.g.) a bird, but not one that I know; or, I see an object that is completely unfamiliar to me. – Perhaps, however, I know all the objects, but I do not understand – in another sense – their arrangement.

### <span class="fac">[12\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/12.webp),[13\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/13.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Suppose the picture depicted people & horses & they were about 1 foot high. Suppose now that I know people & horses of this size, so I could see the picture as their life-size depiction; & it would now make a different impression on me than the one I am used to; although the three-dimensional appearance of the figures (in the picture) would be the same. And yet the impression of the picture is independent of the visual impression that a real person made. I mean: the visual impression of the picture does not include, for example, the memory of the size of real people.

### <span class="fac">[13\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/13.webp),[14\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/14.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> This seeing of the painted figures _as people_ & not as dwarfs is, in a certain respect, analogous to seeing the figures as spatial forms, rather than as flat. It is entirely inappropriate to say that we sometimes grasp what we see in one way, sometimes in another, but always see the same thing.

### <span class="fac">[14\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/14.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> And similarly, when we read a sentence with understanding & without understanding. (Remember what it is like when one reads a sentence with incorrect emphasis, and therefore does not understand it, & then suddenly realizes how it should be read.)

### <span class="fac">[14\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/14.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> When looking at a scrawled script, one can recognize what it means to see something in the given picture.

### <span class="fac">[14\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/14.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Seeing a clock as a clock, i.e., as a dial with hands, is similar to: seeing the Orion as a walking man.

### <span class="fac">[14\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/14.webp),[15\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/15.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> Think of the difference in the understanding of the sentence – when, in it, _this_ word is considered to belong to _this_ one, and sometimes to _that_ one. And I could also have said that it is sometimes understood, seen, or spoken as belonging to this word, sometimes to that one.

### <span class="fac">[15\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/15.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> We could imagine a Martian who gradually learns to understand the facial expressions of people on Earth, and, for example, learns to recognize the threatening one after certain experiences. Until then, he looked at the shape of the face, as we look at the shape of a stone.

### <span class="fac">[15\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/15.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Can I not say: he only _learns_ to understand the commanding gesture in a certain sentential form?

### <span class="fac">[15\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/15.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> We understand Chinese gestures as little as we understand Chinese sentences.

### <span class="fac">[15\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/15.webp),[16\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/16.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> Knowing what the sentence says can only mean: being able to answer the question, “What does it say?”.

### <span class="fac">[16\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/16.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Understanding the sense of a sentence can mean: being able to answer the question “What is its sense?”.

### <span class="fac">[16\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/16.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “Understanding,” by this I mean a correlate of explanation. – It is the opposite of “misunderstanding.” By “misunderstanding” I mean (essentially) something that can be eliminated by explanation. I would not call another kind of disagreement a “misunderstanding.”

### <span class="fac">[16\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/16.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3 = 3˙2</span> For a long time, I was inclined to believe that “_understanding_” is a _metalogical_ word.

### <span class="fac">[16\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/16.webp),[17\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/17.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> “I am not just _saying_ this, I mean something by it.” – Should one ask: “What?” – then another sentence comes as an answer. – Or was the question “what?” inappropriate, because the sentence was only meant to say: “I am not just _saying_ this, something else is also going on in me.” (Similar to the statement: “I am not just reciting the poem, I am also singing it.”)

### <span class="fac">[17\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/17.webp),[18\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/18.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The question is: Can he communicate this _movement_ to me; describe it, other than through the description: “the movement that this, or another, sentence evokes”? Can he compare his meaning with my meaning in any other way than through the comparison of the expression? (And what does that mean: what does one understand by the comparison of two opinions?)

### <span class="fac">[18\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/18.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “Is understanding not something different from the expression of understanding? Is it not the case that the expression of understanding is only an imperfect expression?” – That is, an expression that leaves something out – which is essentially inexpressible. Because otherwise a better one could be found. So the expression would be a perfect _expression_.

### <span class="fac">[18\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/18.webp),[19\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/19.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> What does it mean: “My tears, my face, my words can never communicate to you how sad I am”? What does it mean: ‘to communicate’? – “Words are just words, they cannot communicate a thought.” One can communicate the taste of a dish through words, but also by letting the other taste it. One could call it: “communicating what I feel,” if one knocked out the other’s tooth. Is it now correct to say: “Only _in this way_ can I communicate to him the pain I feel, not through words”? What is the criterion for it being a good communication?

### <span class="fac">[19\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/19.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> I understand this picture perfectly, I could represent it in sound. – I understand this description perfectly, I could draw a picture based on it. In many cases, one could establish as a criterion for understanding that one must be able to represent the sense of the sentence graphically. I am thinking of an official test of understanding. In our difficulties, it is often good to think of such officially (i.e., in practical life) established tests. How is a person tested to see if he can read maps, if he understands the map?

### <span class="fac">[19\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/19.webp),[20\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/20.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Does it follow from this that “understanding” is the activity – drawing, for example – by which we demonstrate our understanding? I once thought so: but this question is, of course, _misleading_! It does not mean: “Is understanding _this_ activity – is it not, perhaps, a _different_ one?” – it only asks: “Is ‘understanding’ used to designate this activity – is it not used _differently_?”

### <span class="fac">[20\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/20.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> It is peculiar: we would like to explain our understanding of a gesture by translating it into words, and the understanding of words by translating it into gestures. (Thus, we are tossed back and forth when we want to find out where understanding actually lies.) And indeed, we explain words by means of a gesture, and a gesture by means of words.

### <span class="fac">[20\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/20.webp),[21\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/21.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Must I understand an order in order to act on it? – Of course! Otherwise, you wouldn’t know what you have to do. But from _knowing_ to acting is again a leap!

### <span class="fac">[21\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/21.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The sentence: “I must understand the order before I can act on it” naturally has a good sense, but again no metalogical one.

### <span class="fac">[21\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/21.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The idea one has here of understanding is, for example, that it brings one closer from the words to the execution. – In what sense is this correct?

### <span class="fac">[21\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/21.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> “But I must understand an order in order to act on it.” Here, the “_must_” is suspicious. – Also, think of the question: “How long before obeying must you understand the order?”

### <span class="fac">[21\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/21.webp),[22\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/22.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> If one were to say that the person receiving the order receives, in addition to the words, images that are similar to the execution of the order (whereas the words are not), I would go even further and assume that the order is given by making the other person perform, through mechanical influence, the movements that he is to perform in 5 minutes according to the order; in this lies the order; and I cannot possibly get closer to the execution of the order in its expression. Then we have replaced the similarity of the image with a much greater similarity. And the path from the sign to the execution seems very shortened here. It also shows how images of fantasy, images are inessential to thought.

### <span class="fac">[22\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/22.webp),[23\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/23.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “There is a gap between the order and the execution. It must be closed by understanding.” “Only in understanding do we have to do _that_. The order (itself) is just sounds, inkstrokes. –”

### <span class="fac">[23\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/23.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> So must I first give the order an _interpretation_? – But what prompts me to _this_ interpretation in particular? Is it the _order_, then it was already sufficient, since it commanded this interpretation. – Or did you arbitrarily add the interpretation – then you are not obeying the order, but what you have made of it.

### <span class="fac">[23\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/23.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> An ‘interpretation’ is something that can be given in signs. It is this interpretation in contrast to another (which sounds different). – If one wanted to say: “every sentence requires further interpretation,” that would mean: no sentence can be understood without an addition.

### <span class="fac">[23\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/23.webp),[24\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/24.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> “I cannot execute the command because I do not understand what you mean. – Yes, now I understand you.” – What was happening when I suddenly understood the other person? There were many possibilities. The command – for example – could have been spoken with incorrect emphasis, & suddenly the correct emphasis occurred to me. I would then say to a third person: “now I understand him, he means…” & would repeat the command with the correct emphasis. And with the correct emphasis I now understand it; i.e., I no longer have to grasp some other, ghostly sense, but the completely familiar German wording is enough for me – Or, the command was given to me in understandable German, but it seemed nonsensical to me because I misunderstood it in some way; then an explanation occurs to me: “ah, he means…”, & now I can execute it. Or several interpretations could have occurred to me, and I finally decide on one of them.

### <span class="fac">[24\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/24.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What does it mean to understand that something is a command, if one does not yet understand the command itself? (“He means: I should do something – but I do not know _what_ he wants.”)

### <span class="fac">[25\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/25.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> To interpret a sign, to give it an interpretation – is a process that probably happens in some, but certainly not in all, cases when a sign is understood.

### <span class="fac">[25\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/25.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> If someone asks me: “What time is it?”, does a process of interpretation now take place in me? I reacted immediately to what I see & hear.

### <span class="fac">[25\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/25.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> The absent-minded person, who, upon hearing the command “Turn right!”, turns to the left, & now, touching his forehead, says: “ah, so – turn right”, & turns right. – Did an _interpretation_ occur to him?

### <span class="fac">[25\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/25.webp),[26\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/26.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> I interpret the words; certainly; but do I also interpret the expressions? _Do_ I, for example, _interpret_ a facial expression as threatening, or friendly? – It _can_ happen. If I were to say: It is not enough that I perceive the threatening face; rather, I must first interpret it. – Someone draws a knife, and I say: “I understand this as a threat.”

### <span class="fac">[26\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/26.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Can one command someone to understand a sentence? Why is it nonsense to say: “Understand this!”? But if someone says to me: “understand this Greek sentence!”, I can still obey the command by learning Greek, & then understand it. Is it only because it cannot be carried out without further ado that one does not say “understand this!”? But one can still command someone who has never learned to play the flute: “Play this piece!”. Is it not this: that “understanding” is not an _activity_? (“Bring about pain!” – not: “Have pain!”)

### <span class="fac">[26\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/26.webp),[27\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/27.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> It is remarkable that when we think that it should now be 3 o’clock, we usually do not imagine the position of the hands exactly, or not at all – but the image, as it were, lies in a toolbox of language, from which we know that we can pull it out at any time if we need it.

### <span class="fac">[27\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/27.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> It is as if I had prepared tools for future use in the toolbox of language – in the grammar, for example.

### <span class="fac">[27\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/27.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> What does it mean to say: “I do not see any violet here, but if you give me a box of paints, I can show it to you in it”? How can one _know_ that one can show it, if …; that one can therefore recognize it when one sees it?

### <span class="fac">[27\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/27.webp),[28\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/28.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> How do I know what the color really looks like by _imagining_ it? How do I know that I will be able to do something? That is, how do I know that what I am doing now is: being able to do _that_?

### <span class="fac">[28\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/28.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “The image must be more than just a picture! Because no matter how similar I make the picture to what it is supposed to represent, it could still be a picture of another object – but the image, in a sense, contains within itself the fact that it is the image of _that_ and of nothing else.” One might come to the conclusion that the image should be seen as a kind of super-image.

### <span class="fac">[28\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/28.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> What does it mean when I give someone the command: “Imagine a red spot,” and then say that understanding the command means knowing what it is like when it is carried out; or even being able to imagine what it is like when…?

### <span class="fac">[28\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/28.webp),[29\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/29.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Compare the different types of lines on a map with the parts of speech in a sentence. The uneducated person sees a lot of lines and does not know that they have very different meanings; they are roads, borders, contour lines, meridians, hatchings, etc. Imagine that a path is drawn on the map and _crossed out_ with some lines – to indicate that the path existed, but no longer exists. –

### <span class="fac">[29\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/29.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> We say: the essential thing about a word is its meaning; we can replace the word with another word that has the same meaning. In this way, a place for the word is fixed, as it were, and one can put one word in place of the other, if one puts it in the same place.

### <span class="fac">[29\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/29.webp),[30\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/30.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Suppose I wanted, all at once, to replace all the words in my language with other words; how could _I_ know in which place one of the new words is? Are the images what hold the place of the words? Or: if I remember the place, what is it that I am remembering? Suppose I had not introduced any new words, but had only changed the places of the old words. –

### <span class="fac">[30\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/30.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The place of a word in grammar is its meaning.

### <span class="fac">[30\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/30.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Would it not be similar if I decided to change the shapes of the chess pieces; or to take the knight as the king, etc.? – But what if I also wanted to change the chessboard? –

### <span class="fac">[30\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/30.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> When I say: “the color of this object is called ‘violet’,” I must already have named the color with the words “the color of this object,” I must already have christened it, in order for the act of naming to take place. Because I could also say: “the name of this color is to be determined by you,” and the one who gives the name must now already know ‘to whom he is giving it’ (in what place in the language he is placing it).

### <span class="fac">[31\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/31.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The uniformity of the application of the word “_meaning_,” compared to the uniformity of the application of “_to show_.” To point to the book, to point to its color, to its shape, to its thickness, to its tastelessness, etc.

### <span class="fac">[31\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/31.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> I am inclined to say: I ‘point’ _in a different sense_ to this body, its shape, its color, etc. – What does that mean? –

### <span class="fac">[31\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/31.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> What does it mean: I ‘hear’ _in a different sense_: the piano, its sound, the piece of music, the one who plays it, his familiarity with it? I ‘marry’ in a different sense: a woman, and her money.

### <span class="fac">[31\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/31.webp),[32\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/32.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> Is the meaning, the understanding of the word, laid down in the explanation of the meaning; or is it only brought about by it, as the pain is brought about by the poison? How does the explanation affect the understanding? – How does the explanation work? That is: what does _it_ bring about; and how does _one apply it_?

### <span class="fac">[32\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/32.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What is the understanding of a word – the _sensations_ that I have when I hear the word – or the _ability_ to apply it? What is the love of A for B: a sensation that A has, – or the willingness to stand up for B, to help him, etc.? Instead, let us ask: How is the word “understanding” (or “love”) actually used?

### <span class="fac">[32\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/32.webp),[33\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/33.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> ‘The meaning of a word is what the explanation of the meaning explains.’ That is: If you want to understand the use of the word “meaning,” then look above all at what one calls “explanation of the meaning.” For this is a concrete thing & does not so easily lead us astray, as the word “meaning” itself, which is suited to send us on a hunt for a shadow.

### <span class="fac">[33\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/33.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> How does this happen, that the words “_that_ is blue” are sometimes meant as a statement about the object one is pointing at, and sometimes as an explanation of the word “blue”? In the latter case, does one really mean: “_that_ is called ‘blue’.” – Can one then mean “is” as “is called” and the word “blue” as “‘blue’,” and at other times mean “is” as “is” in reality? But there is a difference between the case when we want to give someone a communication: “that is blue,” and the case when we want to explain the word “blue” to him, using the same words. What is the difference in such cases? It can also happen that someone draws a word-explanation from a communication.

### <span class="fac">[33\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/33.webp),[34\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/34.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> How can one ask for the explanation of a word-meaning? – For example, like this: “What color is called ‘chrome yellow’?” or: “Which is the three-dashed C?”; but also like this: “What does the word ‘nefas’ mean?” The first and perhaps the second question can be answered by pointing, and the question also presupposed this. The third question could be answered by a translation (or also by examples of the application). – But what if someone who is not mathematically educated asked: “What does the word ‘integral’ mean?” One would probably have to answer: That is a mathematical expression, which I can only explain to you if you understand more mathematics. When I was a child, I once asked someone for the meaning of the word “something.” The answer was: “You don’t understand that yet.” But how could one have explained it? By a definition? Or would one have had to say that the word is indefinable? I don’t know how I later learned to understand it; but I probably learned to apply phrases in which the word occurs. I listened and observed how the adults used it, and I imitated them.

### <span class="fac">[34\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/34.webp),[35\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/35.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> ‘The explanation of the meaning is always only a hint. In the end, he must, and indeed can only, _guess_ what I mean.

(I can – as it were – guide his _hand_, but not his mind.’ – But you are using the word “hint” incorrectly. A “hint” is something that one can complete. And do you think of a completion for this ‘hint,’ and what does it look like? And if the other person is to guess the meaning, then he can probably guess _this_, or _that_ (he can also guess incorrectly sometimes). And how is this expressed: _what_ does he guess? What do you take as a sign for this? And doesn’t the use of language ultimately depend on these signs? What concept do you have of his guessing and understanding the meaning? How do you yourself guess a meaning, _what_ do you guess?

### <span class="fac">[35\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/35.webp),[36\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/36.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Misunderstanding – lack of understanding. Explanation helps against misunderstanding; training helps against lack of understanding – if he lets himself be trained. Why can one not teach a cat to fetch? Does it not understand what one wants? And what is the difference between understanding and not understanding here? (I once saw on a farm how a young wolf-dog wanted to play with a pig. It ran a short distance, lay down ready to jump up, barked – (it wanted to ask the pig to run after it). The pig turned its head once at the barking and then went on its way. It had not understood anything.)

### <span class="fac">[36\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/36.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “That which weighs 1 cm³ of water has been called ‘1 gram.’” – “Yes, but what does it weigh?” “What this sentence says, I can also express _this_ way: ‥‥‥․” – “Yes, but what does it say?”

### <span class="fac">[36\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/36.webp),[37\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/37.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> One might wish to provide an explanation of the _mechanism of meaning_ through memory and association. –

But we feel that an explanation of a mechanism cannot be what matters to us. For that is again a description of phenomena in language. It says, for example: when we hear the word “red,” then the & the idea springs to mind. Well, when that happens—what next? We do not want to hear an explanation of a mechanism, but rather an explanation of a calculus.

### <span class="fac">[37\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/37.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> When I say that the symbol is what produces this effect, the question arises: how can I speak of this effect if it is not present? And how do I know that it _is the one I meant_ when it occurs? It is therefore no explanation that resolves our difficulties to say: “very simple, we compare the fact with the memory-image”—because comparison presupposes a certain method of comparison, & how is that given to us?

### <span class="fac">[37\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/37.webp),[38\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/38.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> How is he to know which color to choose when he hears “red”?—Very simple: he should take the color whose image comes to mind when he hears the word. But how is he to know which color that is, “whose image comes to mind”? Does this require a further criterion? (Incidentally, there _is_ a game: choose the color that comes to mind when hearing the word ….) “‘Red’ means the color that comes to my mind when I hear the word ‘red’” is a _definition_. It does not provide an explanation of what the essence of meaning is. (This refers to what Frege—and occasionally Ramsey—said about recognition as the condition of symbolization. What, then, is the criterion for knowing that I have correctly recognized the color? Perhaps something like the experience of joy upon recognition?)

### <span class="fac">[38\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/38.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The psychological – trivial – discussions about expectation, association, etc., always leave out what is actually remarkable, & one can tell that they are beating around the bush without touching the crucial point.

### <span class="fac">[39\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/39.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “Why do you ask for explanations? When these are given, you will be at an end again. They cannot take you any further than you are now.”

### <span class="fac">[39\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/39.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The effect of a sentence on the mind is not its sense. That is to say: that is not _how_ the word “sense” is used. The investigation of whether the meaning of a sign is its effect is a grammatical investigation. If we give someone a push so that he goes away & he staggers back, we do not _call_ that the sense of the push.

### <span class="fac">[39\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/39.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Is the sense of the request “Give me an apple” its _effect_? Do we say that we meant the request differently if he gives me an apple, and differently if he does not give me one? Did we ask him for something _different_ each time; or was it not a request in _one_ case?

### <span class="fac">[40\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/40.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> When reading a sentence in one’s native language, one sees the individual words as they follow one another, each in a different way. We completely forget that “not” & “me” & “sees,” as spoken or written forms, do not differ _significantly_ from one another. In a language entirely foreign to us, we clearly see the essential uniformity of the words. – Couldn’t one say something quite analogous about the figures of a game? But also, for example, about tools—such as a dentist’s instruments; whoever does not know their purpose sees (only) longer & shorter, sharp & blunt, thick & thin rods. For the one who uses them, each one has its own specific _meaning_ above all else.

### <span class="fac">[40\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/40.webp),[41\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/41.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Insofar as (according to James) there is an if-feeling, an or-feeling, etc., there is certainly (also) a king-feeling, a bishop-feeling, etc. for the chess player. But are these the feelings that actually matter in a game of chess?

### <span class="fac">[41\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/41.webp),[42\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/42.webp),[43\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/43.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “It is rightly regarded as a criterion for understanding the word ‘red’ that one can pick out a red object from others on command; on the other hand, correctly translating the word into English or French is not proof of understanding. Therefore, the red sample is a primary sign for red, while every word is a secondary sign.” What is the proof of understanding: that one can give an explanation of its meaning, or that one can apply it correctly? (I.e., what do you want to call proof of understanding?) And what is our criterion for whether someone can apply the word correctly? Compare this with: who should we say can multiply – etc.? Isn't the ostensive explanation also something that can be _applied_? – Can a red sample only be used in _one_ way as a sample for a color? Isn’t it conceivable that one tries in vain to teach someone the use of the word “red” by pointing to something red, and that it only succeeds when one gives him a blow on the nose when saying this word – “Yes, but only if the blow produces a subjective red visual impression in him!” – I can accept that, – but why should I make any assumption about it? And if a command says: “Imagine a red spot there” – do I make the transition from the word “red” to the image only through a color sample? And if I have such a sample at hand – am I more certain that _this_ is the right color than that my image, which I formed independently of the sample, is the right one? When I write from dictation or read something written: do I make the transition from sound to letter, or from letter to sound, through a sample? – “Yes, but you can’t read the sound from the letter if you don’t already _know_ that this and that sound corresponds to the letter.” – Consider: what does this knowledge consist of? “But is this supposed to mean that the sample – the red square, for example – is just as _arbitrary_ a sign as the word ‘red’?” – If I wanted to communicate with someone whose language I don't understand, and, say, wanted to obtain a red object, I would definitely point to something red in order to achieve mutual _understanding_. (This sign would always be the same, but the _words_ for this color would not.) And copying a written template is something different than writing from dictation. – It is _true_ that there are _various_ things that we call ‘signs’, and various ways of using them. Only the hasty, silly classification is incorrect, which is based on a misunderstanding of our task. (Braithwaite)

### <span class="fac">[43\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/43.webp),[44\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/44.webp),[45\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/45.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> One can use a small red panel as a model for painting a _reddish_ white, or a reddish yellow (etc.)—but can one also use it as a model for painting a shade of blue-green (e.g.)?—What if I saw someone, with all the outward signs of exact copying, ‘reproducing’ a red spot as blue-green?—What if I saw someone, with all the outward signs of exact copying, ‘reproducing’ a red spot as blue-green? – I would say: “I don’t know how he does it!”, or also: “I don’t know _what_ he’s doing.” – But suppose he ‘copied’ this shade of red on various occasions into precisely this blue-green, & other shades of red with regularity into other shades of blue-green – should I now say he is copying, or that he is not copying? – No, as you want. But what does it mean that I don’t know ‘what he’s doing’? Don’t I see what he’s doing? But I can’t see _inside him_. – Just not this simile! If I see him copying red into red, what do I know then? Do I know _how_ I do it? Of course, one says: I’m just painting the _same_ color. – But what if _he_ says: “& I’m painting the fifth to this color”? Do I see a specific process of mediation when _I_ paint the ‘same’ color? Suppose I know this person as an honest person; he renders, as I’ve described, a red through a blue-green—but now _not_ always the same shade through the same one, but sometimes through one shade, sometimes through another. Should I say, “I don’t know what he’s doing”? – He’s doing what I see – but _I_ would never _do_ it; I don’t know why he does it; his way of acting ‘is incomprehensible to me’.

### <span class="fac">[45\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/45.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> When I go through French vocabulary to see if I understand it; isn’t it proof to me that I understand “rouge” if I can translate it as “rot”? – “Yes; but only because you can already apply the German word.” – But doesn’t that also apply to the red pattern when I point to it to show my understanding?

### <span class="fac">[46\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/46.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Is the ‘primary sign’ unambiguous?

### <span class="fac">[46\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/46.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> We sometimes say: “Yes, if the word has _a meaning_ (is supposed to have a meaning), then the sentence is true.” – What do we see here as the word “_that_”?

### <span class="fac">[46\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/46.webp),[47\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/47.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The concept of the ‘secondary sign’ is this: A sign is secondary if, in order to orient myself by it, I need a kind of table that connects it to another (primary) sign, & it is only through this that I can orient myself by the secondary one. The table does not guarantee the uniformity of all the transitions I make, for it does not force me to use it in the same way every time. It is there, like a field through which paths lead, but I can also go cross-country. What, then, is the nature of my statement about the table: that it does not force me to use it this way or that? This explanation brings _to mind_ a use of the table—one I might not have thought of. I could say: “See your theory _this_ way—are you still satisfied with it?”

### <span class="fac">[47\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/47.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Grammar—one might say—is the ledger of language; from which everything must be discernible that concerns the _transactions_ carried out by means of our language;—and not (merely) some feelings about the meaning of words.

### <span class="fac">[47\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/47.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> How do you use the word, what do you do with it—that will teach me how you understand it. (This applies particularly to the ‘interpretations’ of mathematical theorems.)

### <span class="fac">[47\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/47.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> An interpretation of a sentence is its context in grammar.

### <span class="fac">[48\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/48.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Do not speak to me of interpretations, illustrations, or applications of sentences whose precise _relation_ to these sentences you do not provide an explanation for—and do not consider the effort of providing an explanation worth it—because they do not actually pertain to the matter at hand—even though it is precisely they that make your sentences of interest to you.

### <span class="fac">[48\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/48.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> What is a sentence? What defines this concept? – How is this word, “sentence,” used in non-philosophical language? “Sentence”—in contrast to what?

### <span class="fac">[48\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/48.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> When we say: “A sentence is any sign by which we _mean_ something,” the question is: _what_ do we mean—and when do we mean it? While the sentence is being spoken? etc., etc.

### <span class="fac">[48\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/48.webp),[49\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/49.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> When I ask: “What is the general form of the sentence?”—one might counter-ask: _Do_ we even _have_ a general concept of the sentence? One that we now want to define precisely.

### <span class="fac">[49\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/49.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What does the person who constructs (invents) a new language do? According to what principle does he proceed? For this principle will show us what our concept of ‘language’ is.

### <span class="fac">[49\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/49.webp),[50\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/50.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> This question is fundamental: When we learn a new experience—such as a new taste or a new feeling—how do I know that what will describe this experience is a sentence? Or: why should I now call it a sentence? – Well, by the same right with which I speak of ‘describing’ or of a new ‘experience’. – But why did I use the word “experience”—in contrast to what? (Have I, then, already characterized what is about to happen to such an extent that I said it is an experience?) And if I say: but language can expand, after all, the answer is: Certainly, but if this word “expand” has any sense here, I must already know what I mean by it, how I imagine an expansion. And what I cannot think of now, I cannot express now, nor even hint at. In such cases I would like to say: “I cannot, after all, transcend my (own) thinking while thinking!” – – But what I “cannot” do in _that_ sense—why should I even speak of it? (“Calm down,” I might say, “the impossible isn’t going to happen anyway.”) – So let us examine _what_ we do, how we use our words!

### <span class="fac">[50\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/50.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Here we have this nagging problem: how is it even possible to have a thought about such a thing! To even think of the existence of things when we always see only representations—their images.

### <span class="fac">[50\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/50.webp),[51\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/51.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “How could I have even come up with that thought!” ought to mean: What (substance) could there possibly be in this thought? – But to see _that_, we must see how we use it.

### <span class="fac">[51\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/51.webp),[52\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/52.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “How can it make sense to speak of a kind of sensory perception entirely new to me, which I might perhaps have one day—if you do not want to speak of the sensory **organ**?” I have often said such things in discussions (with Ramsey, for example). The reply was then of the sort: “It is,_ after all_, possible to think of such a thing!” (Just as one says: “The technique today does things you can’t even imagine.”) – – Well, what is to be done? One must find out _what_ you mean by that. (That you assure me this phrase is _conceivable_—what can I do with that? That’s not (the) point, after all. Its purpose isn’t (to) create fog in your soul.) _What_ do you mean—how is it to be figured out? We must patiently examine how this sentence is to be interpreted. What everything _around it_ looks like. – Then its sense will become clear.

### <span class="fac">[52\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/52.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “How can my (own) thoughts lead me to a place where there is no _path_ for them to go? That would be (indeed) like saying that a locomotive, if it only has fuel, can travel as far as it likes, & forgetting that it cannot travel farther than the tracks extend.”

### <span class="fac">[52\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/52.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “What concept do I have of the new sensory experience? Do I have one at all? Isn’t ‘sensory experience’ just as empty as ‘event’? ‘An event occurred’—do you now know anything about _what_ happened?”—But if I have no concept, then I won’t be able to do anything with this word either. And what I can do with it will show what concept is there. So do not say: “Your concept is empty,” but rather: “Let us see what your concept is; for it is not immediately apparent _what_ is in it.”

### <span class="fac">[53\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/53.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “I cannot, after all, in thought, through words, gain a glimpse of something I _do not know_.

_(Nihil est in intellectu …)_

As if I could, in thought, come from behind, so to speak, & catch a glimpse of something I cannot see from the front.” – Against whom am I directing myself there? – If I am not mistaken myself – then against a false, i.e., misleading, _conception_, against false, misleading, images. (The misleading juxtaposition: ‘to recognize something through thinking, to grasp it through the intellect’ – ‘to grasp something through sensory perception.’ The intellect, as a kind of _sense_ for nonsensical things.)

### <span class="fac">[53\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/53.webp),[54\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/54.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> I could say: You have a wrong conception; – but the matter cannot be clarified by arguing against your words; but only by trying to direct your attention away from certain _words_, illustrations, images, & towards the _use_ of the words **hin**.

### <span class="fac">[54\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/54.webp),[55\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/55.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> You speak as if the thought—of which you have a vague notion—could accomplish something unheard of; & one must simply acknowledge that—it is, after all, the _thought_. Hardy says in the essay “Mathematical Proof”: “That ‘the finite cannot understand the infinite’ should surely be a theological & not a mathematical war-cry.” It is true, this is a clumsy expression. But what those who use it want to say is: “Surely things must be in order here! Where does this leap from the finite to the infinite come from?” And that way of expressing it is not entirely nonsensical—only that “finite,” which the infinite is not supposed to be able to conceive, is not “man” or “the mind,” but _symbolism_, calculus. And _how_ this “finite” conceives the “infinite”—this is certainly worth investigating. And such an investigation is comparable to the precise examination and clarification of a company’s business conduct by an auditor. The goal is a surveyable representation of all applications, illustrations, and interpretations of calculus. A _comprehensive_ examination. (For any one-sided examination also casts a shadow.) A complete overview of everything that can create ambiguity. And this overview must extend over a wide scope, for the roots of our ideas reach far. Such an investigation is _difficult_. – “The finite does not have the understanding of the infinite” means: It cannot be as you, in your characteristic superficiality, portray it. Thought can, as it were, _fly_; it _need_ not walk. You do not have understanding—that is, you do not grasp—your own actions, and you project, as it were, your lack of understanding into the idea of a medium in which the most astonishing things are possible.

### <span class="fac">[56\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/56.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> One says: “Is there a body here?” – & the criteria are precisely the sensory perceptions. But _this_ is a _new_ construct, a construct of philosophy: “Is there a body behind these sensory perceptions—which were, after all, the criteria for the body’s existence?” –

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-116-56.2) <span class="fac">[56\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/56.webp)</span> {#562}

<span class="series-number">2</span> The strange similarity between a philosophical inquiry (perhaps especially in mathematics) and an aesthetic one. (E.g., what is wrong with this dress, how it ought to be, etc.)

### <span class="fac">[56\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/56.webp),[57\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/57.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> ‘Nothing about itself leads us beyond itself; and neither does any argument.’ – What is this supposed to mean; who believes that this is the case? – Is it _so_: In general, when developing the use of our words & symbols, we don’t need to be too careful; the technique of their use expands entirely on its own, & we don’t notice that something new is happening. – But then there are cases where the path suddenly narrows, where the most serious misunderstandings lie on all sides, & caution is needed. Here one can no longer (carelessly) follow a routine of linguistic usage – but must ask at every step: is this still the old game, has the environment of this expression not _essentially_ changed? Look around! The sign is the cart you are riding in; but is there still a _way_?

### <span class="fac">[57\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/57.webp),[58\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/58.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> He who points out that a word has been used in different meanings, or that when using this expression, this image is present in our minds, & who generally establishes rules according to which words are used, does not take on the obligation to give an explanation of the word “rule” (or “word”, or “language”, or “sentence”, etc.). – And rules that deal with the word “rule” are not meta-rules. That is, things are quite comfortable in philosophy; we are not building a (unique) monumental structure, _the_ right _language_, but we only want to eliminate misunderstandings.

### <span class="fac">[58\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/58.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> How does one distinguish rules from games, for example, from what is not a rule? One sometimes uses the sentence: “_This_ is a rule, _that_ is not.” Does one mean to say that the _one_ has the property that all rules have in common, which is what makes them rules; and that this property is lacking in the other?

### <span class="fac">[58\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/58.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> In some ball games, one draws a line in the middle of the playing field to separate the parties, but does not further limit the game, since this is not necessary.

### <span class="fac">[59\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/59.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> No more concerns about language are justified than a chess player has about the game of chess, namely none.

### <span class="fac">[59\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/59.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> It seems that I am making things easier & easier for myself in philosophy; but the difficulty is in making things easier for oneself, while also being conscientious.

### <span class="fac">[59\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/59.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> What is a sentence? – First of all, there is a sentence **sound** in our languages. (Hence Lewis Carroll's nonsense poems.) And when we say in different places that saying this & that makes no sense, then what makes no sense is never an arbitrary combination of words or syllables, but something that _seems_ to have sense, something that _sounds_ like a sentence or part of a sentence.

### <span class="fac">[59\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/59.webp),[60\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/60.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> Imagine a new language constructed in such a way: the words & the grammar are those of the German language, but the words in the sentence are in the reverse order. A sentence of this language therefore sounds like a German sentence if one starts at its last word & reads to the first. The expressive possibilities therefore have the same multiplicity as in German. But what we know as sentence sound is gone.

### <span class="fac">[60\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/60.webp),[61\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/61.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Consider the form of expression: “I have as many handkerchiefs as <math class="stacked" display="inline"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mspace width="0.3em"/><mo>+</mo><mspace width="0.3em"/><mtext>2x</mtext><mspace width="0.3em"/><mtext>‒</mtext><mspace width="0.3em"/><mn>3</mn><mspace width="0.3em"/><mo>=</mo><mspace width="0.3em"/><mn>0</mn></math>,” or “The number of my suits is <math class="stacked" display="inline"><mi>n</mi><mspace width="0.3em"/><mtext>und</mtext><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mspace width="0.3em"/><mo>+</mo><mspace width="0.3em"/><mtext>2n</mtext><mspace width="0.3em"/><mo>+</mo><mspace width="0.3em"/><mn>2</mn><mspace width="0.3em"/><mo>=</mo><mspace width="0.3em"/><mn>0</mn></math>.” Does this sentence have sense? It is not immediately apparent. This example shows how something can, at first glance, look just like a sentence that we presumably understand, but which, in reality, is nonsense and is only formed by analogy with meaningful sentences.

### <span class="fac">[61\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/61.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> This gives a splendid example of what it means to understand (mean) a sentence.

### <span class="fac">[61\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/61.webp),[62\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/62.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> How do I do it, so that I always use a word in a meaningful way? Do I always look it up in the grammar? No; the fact that I mean something—what I mean—prevents me from saying nonsense. I talk about ‘sharing an apple’ but not about ‘sharing the color red,’ because when I say ‘sharing an apple,’ I can think of something, imagine something, want something; not when I say ‘sharing a color.’ – It would be more accurate to say that when I use the words “sharing an apple,” I think of something, imagine something, want something; with the words “sharing the color red,” not. But this is only because no sense has been given to these words in our language, i.e., because they are not actually used in a language-game.

### <span class="fac">[62\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/62.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> ‘The fact that I mean something with the words prevents me from saying nonsense,’ probably means: I imagine something with them, I want something with them, I do something with them, I use them for a purpose, and that is precisely what excludes nonsense.

### <span class="fac">[62\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/62.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2 = 61˙2 + 62˙1</span> ‘How do I do it, so that I always use a word correctly, i.e., in a meaningful way? Do I always look it up in the grammar? No; the fact that I mean something—what I mean—prevents me from saying nonsense.’ – How do I do it, mean something with the words? I imagine something, I want something with them, I do something with them; I use them for a purpose.

### <span class="fac">[62\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/62.webp),[63\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/63.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> When do we say that a word expression is nonsense? Roughly, it is like this: When a certain form of word expression is used in a language-game, and there is a temptation to form a (somehow similar) sentence by analogy with the sentences of the language-game, and this now gives rise to all sorts of problems, doubts, false conclusions, because its warrant seems undeniable (as it were, through its uniform), and what distinguishes it from the useful sentences is not apparent; – if we now want to separate this word expression from the others, as a formation that disturbs thinking, as it were, to fence it off, then we say that it is nonsense. However, how it actually proceeds in detail can only be shown step by step in this entire investigation.

### <span class="fac">[63\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/63.webp),[64\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/64.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1 = 61˙2</span> “The sentence ‘I divide red’ is nonsensical; one cannot divide _red_.” – I could, after all, give the sentence “I divide red” a sense (it might say something like “I divide a red body”). How, if I asked: which word, which error, makes the sentence nonsense? Why should it be precisely the word “red”? Here we see that with this sentence, even in its nonsensical form, we are thinking of a very specific system of sentences. That is why one also says: “You cannot divide red,” thus giving an answer; whereas one would not respond to just any combination of words. “You cannot divide red” therefore means: Remember that in the language-game to which the sentence appears to belong, you do not know what to make of it.

### <span class="fac">[64\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/64.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What do we do now if we give the word group “I divide red” a sense? – Yes, we could make quite different things out of it. A sentence of arithmetic, an exclamation, an experiential proposition, etc., etc.

### <span class="fac">[64\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/64.webp),[65\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/65.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> What kind of rules are those that say that certain combinations of words have no sense? Are they analogous to the rules that say there is no valid chess position if two figures stand on the same square at the same time; or if a figure stands on the boundary between two squares? Think of a representation of a journey around the Earth; by a line drawn in the projections of the two hemispheres. I now say: a

![](../graphics/116,65.svg)

line segment that, in such a representation, extends beyond the boundary circle of a hemisphere, like a, _has no sense_. One could also say: nothing has been decided about it.

### <span class="fac">[65\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/65.webp),[66\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/66.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What does it mean to “discover that a sentence has no sense”? And what does it mean: “if I mean something with it, it must have sense”? The first means: not to let oneself be misled by the appearance of a sentence & to investigate its use in the language-game. And: “if I mean something with it” – does that mean something _similar_ to: “if I imagine something with it”? – Well, if the whole purpose of the sentence is that it evokes an image in you, then one can say that it has sense. But usually, that is not all that one demands of the sentence. Often, however, the _image_ leads to further use.

### <span class="fac">[66\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/66.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> If one takes it for granted that human beings delight in their fantasy, one should consider that this fantasy is not like a painted picture or a plastic model, but a complex structure made up of heterogeneous components; signs & images. One would like to say: only the _thought_ can _say_ it, not the sign.

### <span class="fac">[69\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/69.webp),[70\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/70.webp)</span>
