Because “one can” in these sentences means: “Doesn’t one do this & that, & doesn’t one call it this & that?” And if I enter a room where I expected to find others, & there is no one there, – don’t I perhaps say to myself: “I am all alone.” – Why shouldn’t I call that a “communication”? And if it seems strange to me to call this a communication – isn’t it because I don’t _use_ it as a communication? I come into a house, think there will be people inside, but it is empty; I look around in it, & see that it is uninhabited; I say to myself: “It is empty. I can do what I want here” – & now I proceed to do this & that. This is already more of a communication.

### <span class="fac">[139\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/139.webp),[140\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/140.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “I assume that he has an image before him.” – – Could I also assume that this stove has an image before it? And why does this seem impossible? Is the human shape necessary for this? –

### <span class="fac">[140\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/140.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What is this assumption? – Words, & an image. But what do I do with these words & this image? (And the image is very rough: I am thinking of the human head, & of the image in it in a nebulous way.)

### <span class="fac">[140\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/140.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “But this assumption certainly has a good sense!” – Yes; these words & this image have a familiar application in normal circumstances. – But if we assume a case in which this application disappears, then we will, as it were, become conscious for the first time of the nakedness of the words & the image.

### <span class="fac">[140\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/140.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Its application – one could say – is a temporal embedding of the sign.

### <span class="fac">[141\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/141.webp),[142\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/142.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “But if I assume that he has, for example, pain, then I simply assume that he has the same thing that I have had so often!” – This doesn’t get us anywhere. The question is precisely: how do I _apply_ this experience of mine to the case of the other? It is as if you say: “You know what it means: it is 5 o’clock here – then you also know what it means that it is 5 o’clock on the sun; it simply means that it is _just as much_ o’clock there as it is here when it is 5 o’clock here.” The explanation by means of _sameness_ does not work here, because I know that one can call 5 o’clock here “the same time” as 5 o’clock there, but I do not know in which case one speaks of time being simultaneous here & there. It is _just_ like saying that the assumption that he has pain is simply the assumption that he has the same thing as I do. Because _this_ part of the grammar is clear to me, namely that one would say that the stove has the same experience as I do, if one says that he has pain & I have pain.

### <span class="fac">[142\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/142.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What misleads us here is the use of the word “to have”, & the apparent analogy of our case with this: “He has a spot on his nose” – “I have a spot on my nose”. Here, the application to the other is much simpler & there is no danger of being misled.

### <span class="fac">[142\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/142.webp),[143\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/143.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> We always want to say: “A memory-image is a memory-image! Whether _he_ has it, or I have it; and however I come to know whether he has one or not.” – I could agree with that. – And if you ask me: “Don’t you know what I mean when I say he has a memory-image?” – then I can answer: “I can picture something when I hear those words; – but in this specific case, the usefulness of those words doesn’t go any further.” And I can also picture something when I hear the words: “It was exactly 5 o’clock in the afternoon on the sun” – namely, a pendulum clock showing 5 o’clock. – Perhaps an even better example would be the application of “up” & “down” in relation to the globe. Here we all have a _very clear_ idea of the meaning of “up” and “down”. I see that I’m up, the Earth is below me! (Don’t laugh at this example. We’re taught in elementary school that it’s silly to say such things. But it’s just much easier to gloss over a problem than to solve it.) And only upon reflection do we realize that we cannot play the usual game with “up” and “down” here, that we must adapt it if we want to use these words. (So that, for example, we can speak of the antipodes as the people “below” our continent, but we must also acknowledge as correct when they make the same expression applicable to us.)

### <span class="fac">[144\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> You say: “It makes sense – I know what it means!” – & you cling to the familiar words & to an image.

### <span class="fac">[144\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/144.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> It is as if we were doing the other person an injustice if we did not grant him the possibility of the same experience that we had. But we can easily grant it to him, yes, let the most primitive image apply to him – if we only don’t realize that we have misunderstood the function of our language.

### <span class="fac">[144\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/144.webp),[145\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/145.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> It seems so clear here: that ‘_understanding_ the word’ is one thing, & ‘being able to _apply_ it’, another. And this comes again from the fact that we are used to accepting the ostensive explanation as the final answer to the question “Do you understand the word…?” Because it seems as if we can immediately give ourselves the ostensive explanation to the question: “Do you understand what ‘memory-image’ (or ‘pain’ etc.) means,” by conjuring up an image in our mind.

### <span class="fac">[145\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/145.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Think of the different application of these two ‘ostensive explanations’: a) Someone asks: “Who of these people is L.W.?” – One of them points to himself & says: “I am L.W.”.

b) Someone asks me: “What does ‘_ich_’ mean?” I point to myself & say: “This is _ich_.”

### <span class="fac">[145\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/145.webp),[146\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/146.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> It seems, then, as if I can give a kind of ostensive explanation of the word ‘pain’ (for example), just as I could give such an explanation of the word ‘goat’. Only the cases are not quite parallel. I can say: “Oh yes, I know what ‘goat’ means” & conjure up the image of a goat in my mind, but I can also point to a real object; & if I point to a cow, I will be told that I do _not_ know what the word means.

### <span class="fac">[146\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/146.webp),[147\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/147.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “I understand perfectly what it means when he imagines a red circle now, even if I might not be able to figure out whether it is actually the case.” – Is it a state of consciousness when you understand that perfectly? You probably mean: you have an image in your mind when you hear these words. You see the person, imagine a red circle, something, and something floating above him – and if not that, then something similar. We know that there are specific images of such states or processes in the mind of the other person. In a painting, one sees the dreamer lying asleep and, in a way, the ‘dream image’ floating above him. In old silent films, a memory was depicted by a bluish – almost nocturnal – image, which was usually small and _round_ and surrounded by darkness. – One might ask whether a memory really looks like that – and yet everyone understood what this image meant (otherwise, no one would have thought of using this depiction). We simply have – in accordance with the forms of expression of our language – primitive images of the processes in the mind of the other person.

### <span class="fac">[147\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/147.webp),[148\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/148.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Now, _understanding_ very often means: being able to form an image. And the words: “I can form a clear image of it” – are considered synonymous with: “I understand.” For in the cases where the correct transfer of the words into an image guarantees the correct use of the words in other respects. One might speak here of an _act_ of understanding, grasping, and this would then be the translation of the words into an image. However, if you compare such a case with the statement: “I understand what it means when he imagines a red circle,” then it may indeed be true that you form an image of what he imagines, i.e., – for example – a red circle, but the image of the “_having in mind_” of the image is entirely irrelevant for the understanding, i.e., for the further use of the words. Here – one might say – your image _stammers_.

### <span class="fac">[148\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/148.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> So, if you say that you understand perfectly what that statement means, one can answer: You understand it as everyone who understands German understands it under _normal_ circumstances. However, the fact that you have an image in mind when you hear that statement, whatever its nature (an image of how the other person has an image in mind), does not guarantee your understanding of the words.

### <span class="fac">[148\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/148.webp),[149\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/149.webp),[150\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/150.webp),[151\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/151.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Here now, it happens that our thinking plays a curious trick on us. We want to cite the law of the excluded middle & say: “Either such a picture occurred to him, or it didn’t – there is no third possibility!” – Weyl also used this curious argument in the discussion about the occurrence of a certain digit in the infinite development of an irrational number. – ‘In the infinite development of π, there are either five 7s in a row, or there aren’t.’ That is, God knows; but we do not know it. What does that mean? – We use a picture; the picture of a visible series, which one person overlooks, and the other does not. The law of the excluded middle says here: It must either look _like this_, or _like that_. So it actually says – & this is obvious – nothing at all, but gives us a picture – & the problem is now whether reality corresponds to the picture, or not. And this picture now _seems_ to determine what we have to do, how & what we have to look for – but it does not, because we do not know how it should be applied. If we say here “there is no third,” or “there really isn’t a third,” then this expresses that we cannot turn away from this picture that looks as if the problem & its solution must already lie in it, while we _feel_ that this is not the case. Similarly, if one says: “Either he has this sensation, or he does not have it” – then above all a picture appears to us, which already seems to determine the sense of the statements _unambiguously_. “You know now what it is about” – one would like to say. And precisely because of that, he does not know it yet. (In general, the ‘law of the excluded middle’ would most likely be used as follows: We give someone a drawing, for example, & say: “Go there & see if it looks _like this_, or not.” The addition “there is no third,” could then mean: _I_ only want one of these two reports & no third.)

### <span class="fac">[151\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/151.webp),[152\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/152.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Imagine this process: A chain of people is set up, including me, each holding the neighbor by the hand, & I want to assume that they are standing with their arms outstretched. Now, let's say a hot air jet is blown on them, starting with the first, over the chest & arm to the second, and so on, to the last. One can now say that a pain travels through all these people. But I could also say: There are _two_ phenomena here: first, the air jet & the behaviour of the people – a wave, as it were, passes through the entire chain; secondly, there is a sensation of pain that begins when the jet reaches me, passes through me & _stops_ when the jet passes to the next person. – “But this phenomenon does not stop; it just passes to the next person!” – “The phenomenon that _I_ am talking about stops!” – Who is right here? – The one who says that the line still intersects the circle, but at two imaginary points, or the one who says it stops intersecting it? – You can express it _this_ way & _that_ way. And a certain attitude of your gaze will make you inclined to _this_, and another attitude to another form of expression; yes, it will make one or the other form of expression irresistible to you.

### <span class="fac">[152\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/152.webp),[153\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/153.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> I experience that a pain travels from one place to another (namely in my body); & I could also imagine that it travels in the same way to the body of another: in that the region of ‘my’ sensation of pain expands, or that what one otherwise counts as belonging to my neighbor’s body now, _in this respect_, belongs to mine (or: _also_ belongs to mine). But do I not only experience _pain_, do I also experience that _I_ _have_ it? – But what does this question mean?? I am sharing my experience with another person with the words “_I_ have pain”. So what is it that resists this way of expressing myself & says that we are not talking about a subject that _has_ it?

### <span class="fac">[153\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/153.webp),[154\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/154.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Well, a way of expressing myself suggests itself to me in which “pain” appears only where I would normally say “my pain.” And if I adopt such a way of expressing myself, then it really has no sense to speak of “my pain.” It is strange that one is now inclined to say: “Actually, it should read: ‘ …’”. – So one person says: “_Actually_, the line still intersects the circle, …”, while another says: “_Actually,_ of course, it no longer intersects it.” – Similarly: “Actually, one thinks even in sleep—only unconsciously”—“Actually, that’s not really thinking, but …”.

### <span class="fac">[154\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/154.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> And this shows you once again how the word “pain” (for example) can be explained by the _same_ reference, but then used in different ways.

### <span class="fac">[154\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/154.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> I asked: “What is it that rebels against this mode of expression?” – But above all: Usually, nothing in me rebels against this mode of expression at all! Only under very special circumstances—when my gaze is directed in a very special way. Namely, when I am philosophizing and have withdrawn into a kind of contemplation.

### <span class="fac">[154\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/154.webp),[155\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/155.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> If I were now to reserve the word “pain” entirely for what I have hitherto called “my pain,” & what others have called “the pain of L.W.,” this would not do the others any injustice, provided only that a notation were provided whereby the omission of the word “pain” in other contexts is somehow replaced. The others would still be pitied, treated by a doctor, etc. Of course, there would be _no_ objection to this way of speaking if one were to say: “But the others have exactly the same thing that you have!” But what would I gain from this new way of speaking? Nothing. But the solipsist does not _want_ any practical advantages when he defends his view!

### <span class="fac">[155\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/155.webp),[156\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/156.webp),[157\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/157.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> I would like to say: “When I say, ‘I am in pain,’ I am not pointing to a person who is in pain, since in a certain sense I do not even know _who_ is in pain.” And that can be justified. For above all: I did not say that such-and-such a person is in pain, but rather: “I am in pain …”. Now, by that I am not naming any person. Just as little as when I _groan_ in pain. Although the other person can tell from the groaning who is feeling the pain. What does it mean to know _who_ is feeling pain? It means, for example, to know which person in this room is feeling pain: that is, the one sitting there, or the one standing in that corner, the tall one with blond hair over there, or the fat one, etc., etc. – What do I want to say? That there are very different criteria for a person’s ‘identity.’ Now, which one is it that determines me to say, ‘I’ am in pain? None at all. For if I do not see myself (etc.)—with my eyes closed, for instance—(then) I can imagine that I have changed form and location. So when I open my eyes again and find that everything around me has changed; that there is a person sitting there who looks like I used to when I saw myself in the mirror, that my body looks like that of N.N., and that I am standing where I saw him standing just a few seconds ago. Am I still L.W.? That is, if I am in pain and instead of saying, “I have …,” I wanted to say “L.W. has …,” and if someone were to come to the aid of that other person there instead of me—would the others be wrong to act that way? Are they _not_ following the rules of the language-game?

### <span class="fac">[157\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/157.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> But how do I know that I am it?!—That I am _something_? That I am _me_ means nothing. That I am the person who has this _story_—well, that _is_ what I _say_—that is what I remember. But if the others do not accept that, then the situation could well be just as if I were now suddenly ‘_imagining_’ that I had been someone else, i.e., that I had looked completely different & had a different _story_ than the one I have according to everyone else’s statement.

### <span class="fac">[157\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/157.webp),[158\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/158.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “But surely, when you say, ‘I have…,’ you want to draw the attention of others to a specific person.” – The answer could be: No; I only want to draw their attention to _myself_. –

### <span class="fac">[158\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/158.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “But surely, with the words ‘I have…,’ you want to distinguish between _you_ & _the other_.” That is to say: I do not want to say that the other person has pain, _but I_ do. – – I want to say the words that I say, & not other words. But the word “I,” although it stands in the same place in the sentence as “he,” functions differently. Do I know who is speaking when I know that I am speaking? – – –

### <span class="fac">[158\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/158.webp),[159\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/159.webp),[160\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/160.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “If I can have pain, the other person can have it too. Just as I can have it, the other person can have it.” – But this ‘argument’ is simply a movement, a walk around, in our language. One could also say: the argument moves in the pictures that are all too familiar & well-known to us & does not touch the problem, since the problem lies in the application of those pictures. Whereas in one class of cases we strive to find a picture, & once this is found, the application almost takes care of itself, here we already have a picture that forces itself upon us at every turn, but does not help us with the difficulty, which only now begins. For example, if I ask: “How should I imagine that _this_ mechanism goes into _that_ housing?” then, as an answer, a drawing on a smaller scale can serve. One can then say to me: “See, _that’s_ how it goes in”; or perhaps also: “Why are you surprised? Just as you see it _here_, so it goes there too.” The latter, of course, explains nothing more, but only asks you to make the application of the picture that I have given you.

### <span class="fac">[160\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/160.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> But _our_ problem is not: “How am I to _imagine_ that the other person has this or that idea?” – – Rather: How can I imagine it in ways _other_ than the usual way; what _other_ image can I use for this? – namely, to protect myself from misunderstanding the application of the usual image. – Or also: How can I apply the usual image in ways other than the usual way? – so that it becomes clear that there could be other applications as well.

### <span class="fac">[160\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/160.webp),[161\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/161.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> That is why it is important to imagine, for example, the case that I could not only feel pain in my own body, but also in the body of the other. And if I were then told: “the other person has pain” means: he now has what I had before, then I could understand this in such a way: My pain has now changed its location, it has gone from this body into the other. If one were to say to me, then: “You know what it is like when _you_ feel pain – now go from yourself to the other!” then I could make various transitions, & this shows that one cannot say: “_This_ is what is meant by ‘pain,’ & you know what ‘I have,’ ‘you have’ means, etc. – so you know what it means: ‘he has pain.’” (Two misleading pictures.)

### <span class="fac">[161\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/161.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> I see a red spot & say: “It is _I_, after all, who sees this. –” – But, above all, what does “this” mean here? (And it does not mean: ‘what I _mean_ by it.’) But if I leave the meaning of “this” open, where then is the sense of the sentence?

### <span class="fac">[162\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/162.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> One must learn to regard the expression “_I_ have pain” as being as metaphorical as: “the line intersects the circle at two imaginary points.”

### <span class="fac">[162\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/162.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> All the difficulties here arise from the fact that we create images that lead us to expect a different use of the words than the actual one. Here is a continuous conflict.

### <span class="fac">[162\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/162.webp),[163\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/163.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> An image is conjured up that seems to _unambiguously_ determine the sense. The reality of the usage seems somewhat tainted in comparison to what the _image_ shows us. It is the same here as in set theory: the form of expression seems tailored for a God who knows what we cannot know; he sees the entire infinite series and looks into the consciousness of human beings. For us, however, these forms of expression are, so to speak, a set of regalia that we may well don, but with which we cannot do much, since we lack the real power that would give this attire sense and purpose. In the reality of their use, we take detours, so to speak, and walk through side streets; while we may well see the straight, wide road ahead of us, we can never use it because it is permanently blocked.

### <span class="fac">[163\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/163.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Doesn't it all come down to the fact that the words “I have pain” correspond to a groan or a cry? That if I groan out of sympathy, one does not infer from it who is suffering, but a sign (or the like) is needed for that, while the cry of the sufferer leads us to him.

### <span class="fac">[163\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/163.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> I could complain about pains, and not know _who_ has them.

### <span class="fac">[163\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/163.webp),[164\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/164.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> “But you certainly know that _you_ have them and not the other.” – I certainly don't know whether the other has them. And when I say “I have pain,” I simply _complain_; and the one who complains is what one _calls_ “the one who has the pain.” The complainer is the one of whom one says: _he_ has the pain; therefore, one cannot say that the complaint _expresses_ which person has the pain.

### <span class="fac">[164\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/164.webp),[165\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/165.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “But you do use ‘_I_ have’ as opposed to ‘_he_ has’.” – Well, in this case I say “I,” and do not say “he.” And “I” does indeed occupy the same position in the sentence structure as “he” does in other cases. But it is not as if _the_ pointer now points to _me_ (i.e., here: to my body), whereas it otherwise points to someone else. (For it is not the case that _I_ have pain because it is now in _my_ body.) For I am, after all, tempted to say: of the other person I know that he has pain because I observe his behaviour; of myself—because I _feel_ it. But that is precisely senseless, since “I feel pain” means the same as “I have it.” It seems here as if, in one case, _one_ sense helps me find the owner of the pain, and in the other case, the other sense does; just as I can search for an object with my eyes, but also with my ears. And one might well say that in one case the sense of sight leads me to the _location_ of the pain, in the other case the sense of pain; but my sense of pain does not lead me to _the owner_ of the pain. If each of these people “knows” that they are in pain—does each have knowledge of something different? Doesn’t everyone know the same thing, namely: “_I am_ _in_ pain”? – The situation is different with the sentence: “He is in pain” – for “he” refers to a name, a description, or (a) pointing gesture; without such a reference, the expression has no sense. “I” & “he” do not serve the same purposes in our language.

### <span class="fac">[165\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/165.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> One might imagine that someone groaned: “Someone has pain – I don't know who!” – whereupon one hurried to help him, the one groaning.

### <span class="fac">[165\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/165.webp),[166\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/166.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Or imagine: One suddenly lets out cries of complaint and says, pointing to another: “He has pain, he has pain!” But this other gives no signs of pain. Suppose one found that applying cold compresses to him soothed the one complaining, then it would be natural to say that he had pain in the other person's body. In this case, one could _teach_ the complainer to say “I have pain in him” instead of “he has pain.”

### <span class="fac">[166\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/166.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> If several people call out to one person: “Come to me!” – do they want different things, or does each of them want the same thing?

### <span class="fac">[166\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/166.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “But you do say, at any rate, that _you_ are in pain!” – I did not mean to assert that _I_ have something; I only wanted to complain, and I was taught to complain: “I am in pain.” For when I _assert_ “I have …,” I am asserting a relationship between an object and my body. Here, however, my body _does_ indeed _complain_, but the complaint says nothing about it, unless it says that the pain is _within it_.

### <span class="fac">[167\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/167.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The complaint does not say who is complaining.

### <span class="fac">[167\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/167.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> What if one learned to use the words as a complaint: “I complain!” Does one then not only complain, but also say _who_ is complaining?

### <span class="fac">[167\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/167.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Suppose someone is taught to point at people and say: “_There_ is N,” “_There_ is M,” etc., and also: “_There I_ am.” Is the latter taught to him in the same way? Of course, here too he points at a person, but what he was taught was to bend his arm when saying _these_ words.

### <span class="fac">[167\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/167.webp),[168\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/168.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> “I do not feel that _I_ have something; I only feel pain.” “I do not want to say that something is now _with me_; that a something now stands in a special relationship to _my_ body.” “I do not want to say that a something, which I call ‘pain,’ has (now) taken up residence within _me_.” – But after all, instead of the complaint “I have pain,” one could use: “something … has taken up residence …”. – So “I don’t want to say that” means: I don’t use these words _that way_, but _this way_. I am thus drawing attention to a grammatical distinction. I could also say: “Don’t let yourself be misled by the _figurative expression_ ‘I have pain.’”

### <span class="fac">[168\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/168.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “I know that _I_ have pain because I _feel_ it.” seems to be like: “I know that _I_ have the googly-box because I _feel_ it (not because I see it).”

### <span class="fac">[168\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/168.webp),[169\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/169.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “You know, insofar as you know that _you_ have the pain, who has the pain” – seems to be saying something like: “You know, in any case, that the pain is with _you_ now” – just as one says: “I now know where the sack is – because _I_ have it.” – But that means: “Now I am no longer in doubt about who has it—because I have it.” But can one also say: “Now I am no longer in doubt about who is in pain, because I am in pain”? Am I now less in doubt about others, & was I in doubt about myself before?

### <span class="fac">[169\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/169.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “You don’t doubt whether you have it or the other person has it!” – The sentence “I don’t know whether I, or the other person, is in pain” would be a logical product of which one factor would be: “I don’t know whether I am in pain or not”; & this is not a meaningful German sentence.

### <span class="fac">[169\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/169.webp),[170\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/170.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Imagine this: Several people are standing in a circle, including me. One of us, sometimes this one, sometimes that one, is brought into contact with the poles of an electrifying machine without us being able to see it. I try to figure out which one of us is being electrified right now. Once I say: “Now I know who is connected to the induction coil; it’s _me_, after all.” In this sense, I could also say: “Now I know who feels the shocks; it’s me, after all.” This would be a somewhat strange way of expressing it. But if I can also feel the shocks at a location outside my body, so that saying I feel them does not specify which body the contact is touching, then the expression “Now I know who…” seems entirely inadequate.

### <span class="fac">[170\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/170.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> To “I know…” there is a “See, it _is_ so”.

### <span class="fac">[170\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/170.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> An utterance of sensation can be compared to the card that a card player receives. It is the starting position of the game, but not yet the result of it.

### <span class="fac">[170\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/170.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> If you say, “I know that I am in pain,” then someone might reply to you: “Show it, if you are in pain. If you cannot show anything, then you do not know anything either.”

### <span class="fac">[170\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/170.webp),[171\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/171.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> Imagine that before each sentence one said the words “I say”; and its purpose would be to draw the other person’s attention to oneself. Or one clears one’s throat before each sentence, for the same purpose, and the other person has to turn to face the sound. Is then every sentence a statement about the speaker?

### <span class="fac">[171\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/171.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> If one compares feeling pain with observing, then the pain is the observed thing, and one is not observing, in addition, _who_ is feeling it, i.e., _who_ is observing it.

### <span class="fac">[171\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/171.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “Sometimes I observe pain when others have it, sometimes I _feel_ pain.” – But not: “sometimes I feel pain in myself” – unless in contrast to cases in which I feel pain in the bodies of others.

### <span class="fac">[171\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/171.webp),[172\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/172.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> If I say as an introduction: “I say:”, am I first telling him _who_ is speaking? – But if I say: “I speak unclearly,” then I am telling him who is doing this. The sentence is an assertion, an expression of opinion, or of knowledge. The introduction “I speak:” is _not_ that.

### <span class="fac">[172\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/172.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The greeting: “I greet you!” – the reaction to it is not a reaction to an assertion. Therefore, the words “I greet you” and “God bless you” can amount to the same thing.

### <span class="fac">[172\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/172.webp),[173\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/173.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The greeting says nothing about a person. But it does say: “I greet you,” so it does say something about a person, in so far as the wording determines this. However, one can say: these words are not _treated_ here as such a statement or assertion; but as a greeting. For example, they are not _tested_ or _confirmed_ as an assertion. Likewise, the expression of thanks: “I thank you!” Thus, the greeting “How do you do?” is not treated as a question. The words: “I hate you” can simply be the utterance of hatred; but, under circumstances, also an assertion, about the truth of which one can argue.

### <span class="fac">[173\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/173.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> I point and say: “The sky is reddening over there!” – can one ask: “Are you sure that it is reddening _there_?” One could say that the sentence: “A flame appears to me over there” – is not a statement about a place. Rather, it is the utterance of a visual impression. Likewise: “I see _this_ picture over there” (which I now reproduce through a drawing).

### <span class="fac">[173\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/173.webp),[174\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/174.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “I now know who feels the blows; _the one_ over there.” “I now know who feels the blows; _I_.” – One might reply: “Should I take that as an expression of sensation, or have you _observed_ something in yourself?” If that is the signal of sensation (corresponding, for example, to a twitch of the face)—then I know what to make of it; & I do not concern myself with the wording, which seems to suggest a different use.

### <span class="fac">[174\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/174.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Imagine that one looks around in a circle and says, now and then: “Now I know who is feeling the blows: _he_ there.” And now he twitches himself. – Was his twitch an assertion, like those other ones? That is, did he give this sign on the basis of the same preliminary play as those; when he says that he feels the blows, is he still looking for confirmation of his assertion? Does the other person ask him how he knows that…?

### <span class="fac">[174\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/174.webp),[175\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/175.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The “I have” in “I have pain” is the characteristic of the sensation signal. That is, it means something different here than in the _assertions_ “I have…”: where the relationship of something to my body is stated. – The sensation signal is an utterance of my body; but it does _not_ name me, i.e., him, in that it also says nothing about him.

### <span class="fac">[175\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/175.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The complaint does not name the complainant. Although it draws attention to him.

### <span class="fac">[175\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/175.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The statement “I have pain” is a statement about me in a _figurative_ sense. But it is not used as a statement about me, i.e., about my body.

### <span class="fac">[175\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/175.webp),[176\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/176.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> I would like to say: The reference to _me_ in the statement of the sensation is, so to speak, empty. – But it is _not_ empty insofar as the words “I have” have a function in the sentence; they distinguish this case from the one in which (instead of them) “he has” appears. – I feel that the reference is empty when the statement of pain seems to say: “I express pain”. – How, then, can the _complaint_ say _that_ I am complaining?! Well, it can – – & it cannot insofar as one cannot communicate the fact that one is complaining until the complaint has been uttered, & that one does not call the _communication_ that one has complained “complaint.”

### <span class="fac">[176\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/176.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> To call the utterance of a sensation an _assertion_ is misleading _in_ that the word “assertion” is associated in the language-game with the ‘verification of the assertion,’ the ‘confirmation of the assertion,’ and the ‘refutation of the assertion.’

### <span class="fac">[176\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/176.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “I know very well that _I_ am _in pain_!”—You _say_, at any rate, that you know it—and that is enough for me; but the question is: How did you arrive at this statement, what happens to it, what _is_ its _purpose_?

### <span class="fac">[177\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/177.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What, for example, does the statement serve: “I _have_ something when I have pain”?

### <span class="fac">[177\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/177.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> We repeatedly confuse statements of the kind: “I am inclined to call this _this_—not _that_”—with the communication that something behaves this _way_, and not that way. All metaphysical (timeless) statements could be made in the form: “I_ am inclined_ to …”. “I am inclined not to call a statement about what _will_ happen a ‘sentence’,” “I am inclined to call color, size, and location ‘objects’,” “I am inclined to call the number 3 an objective, not a real object.”

### <span class="fac">[177\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/177.webp),[178\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/178.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> How could one decide: whether you have something when you feel pain? Well, you want to say that – but is this the criterion for it being so? You can also use a cogwheel as a paperweight. Then we would say something like: it is not functioning as a cogwheel here.

### <span class="fac">[178\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/178.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> One can say: You ‘_know_’ only what you can verify. You _can_ verify, for example, whether your sensation is called “braking”. “That _is_ called ‘braking’?”, one can say by giving the person asked an electric shock.

### <span class="fac">[178\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/178.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> One could say to the one who expresses his sensation in the words: “I know one who…, namely _myself_” – You are using the words “I know” in a strange way. You have learned to use this expression in a completely different language-technique (otherwise). There was in this game ‘self-conviction’, ‘doubting’, ‘greater & lesser certainty’, ‘confirmation’.

### <span class="fac">[178\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/178.webp),[179\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/179.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Instead of: “one cannot”, say: “there is no such thing in this game”: Instead of “one cannot rochade in checkers” – “there is no rochade in checkers”; instead of “I cannot show my sensation” – “there is no showing of what ‘one has’ in the use of ‘I have the sensation…’”; instead of “one cannot enumerate all cardinal numbers” – “there is no enumeration of all the members here, even if there is an enumeration of members.”

### <span class="fac">[179\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/179.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The sentence “Sensations are private” is of the kind: One plays patience alone.

### <span class="fac">[179\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/179.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “Now _I_ have the pain”—So you know that you now have what _he_ used to have—but how do you know that it is _the same thing_? Question: _What is the comparison method here_? Following on from that: In what sense does one say here that one _has something_?

### <span class="fac">[179\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/179.webp),[180\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/180.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> “I do not choose the mouth that makes the utterance.” – The person of whom it is said that they _have_ the memory, the pain, the visual impression, is the one whose mouth makes the utterance. – I do not decide _in this way_: “I feel a pain; I notice that _I_ have it; so _I_ must also express it.”

### <span class="fac">[180\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/180.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The use of the _utterance_ is not taught to you by having a phenomenon presented to your senses & represented with these words. How does one learn the use of the words: “I imagine …”?

### <span class="fac">[180\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/180.webp),[181\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/181.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Can I doubt whether it was _I_ who, as I recall, sat in the library’s reading room yesterday, or whether it was someone else? – Certainly: by doubting whether I sat there yesterday and whether someone else sat there. – But can you doubt whether the memory you have depicts you or someone else (just as you doubt whether the figure in the photograph is you or someone else)? Well, an image may appear in my memory; I see both of us sitting at the table, but I no longer know whether _I_ was sitting in this seat and you in the other, or whether it was the other way around. Or else: I see myself sitting at this table in my memory. But the image that appears before me is not that of a person—say, in profile—sitting at a table, but rather the image seen by the one who is sitting at the table himself. If I then give this image, as it were, the title: “I, sitting at this table,” it is not because I take a figure in the image to be myself. But that is not how it normally happens at all, that my memory shows me an image, & I judge by the likeness of the portrait whom it depicts. Rather, with the image, or even without one, the words come to me: “I remember having been there & there.” My words are an expression of memory; they are themselves the phenomenon of memory & not merely the description of a memory-image. To doubt whether it is me that my memory shows me means, in this case, to doubt whether this is truly the expression of my memory. One also says: “Do you really remember that …, or only _that_ …?” But what if someone asked: “Are you sure that you remember that it was nice yesterday, and not that it rained?” –

### <span class="fac">[182\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/182.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “‘I’ in my mouth refers to _me_.” – Does this word in _my_ mouth designate something special? I probably meant: “‘I’ always refers to the person who utters it.” But what does that mean, that it refers to him? Is there only _one_ possibility?

### <span class="fac">[182\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/182.webp),[183\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/183.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> It is possible not to know whether this foot, which I see here in front of me, is mine or someone else’s. One could then say: “Touch it, if I feel it, then it is _my_ foot.” I could explain: “Every part of my body belongs to me, in which I can have sensations.” Another definition would be: “My foot is the one that is connected to my body.” Accordingly, I could say: “Take the blanket off our legs, then I will see if that is _my_ foot.” (I assume, for example, that the foot is paralyzed.) “But which one is ‘your body’?” – “_This_ is what I call my body,” while making a _reflexive_ pointing gesture. That is the foot that belongs to these pain sensations. I do not mean: ‘that’, which looks so and so; so that I could show non-reflexively at some other time & say: “My body is now _there_, not _here_ anymore, as before.” I could also have said: “_Here_ is my body,” & that would not have meant: “my body is now here,” but: “the body that is here is called ‘my body’.” One could say: “_my_…” can be defined as _possessive_ or _reflexive_. “That is my foot” can mean: that is the foot that belongs to my mouth; ‘my mouth’ is the mouth that utters these words. That is: _thus_ the words “my mouth” are used in the language-game. The fact is that one does not say: “Let me see which is _your_ mouth, is it this one, or that one?”

### <span class="fac">[183\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/183.webp),[184\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/184.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Consider: How can these questions be applied, & how can they be decided: 1) “Are these books _my_ books?” 2) “Is this foot _my_ foot?” 3) “Is this body _my_ body?” 4) “Is this mouth _my_ mouth?” 5) “Is this sensation **my** sensation?”

For 3): One could point to a picture in the mirror. But the question could also be applied as in № 2. Under certain circumstances, however, one could _feel_ a body, & the question means, under other circumstances, the same as: “Does _it_ look like my body?”

For 5) Which is _this_ sensation; i.e.: how does one use the demonstrative pronoun here? But differently than, for example, in the first example! Confusions arise here again because one imagines pointing to a sensation by directing one’s attention to it.

### <span class="fac">[184\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/184.webp),[185\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/185.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “_This_ is my body.” – Well, & who are _you_? – “_This_ is me.” In both explanations, he makes the same pointing gesture. – But does that mean that “I” means the same as “my body”? Can I say: my _body_ loves, thinks, imagines something, etc.? – Now why not? This way of speaking has not yet been ‘taken’. – But would it be _true_ to say that? – It would be true if the corresponding sentence were true in the currently used way of speaking. But it is not my _body_ that loves, or is happy! – I.e.: It does not ‘feel happy’ in the same sense as it ‘twitches’ or ‘grows’; but _I_ do not ‘feel happy’ in the same sense as _I_ ‘twitch’ or ‘grow’. And it is indeed true that through the form of expression: “my body is happy” – an _utterance_ in appearance would still be (much) more similar to the _assertion_ based on an observation (like: “I have a rash”).

### <span class="fac">[185\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/185.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> ‘Mine’ is what I _have_; & _this_ (with a reflexive gesture) is me. So if you want to know whether something is mine, look to see who possesses it. If you want to know, for example, whether this house is my house, see who bought it, who lives in it, etc. But how if I say: “See if this face is _my_ face?”

### <span class="fac">[185\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/185.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> But is “_This_ is me” (with the reflexive gesture) an explanation at all? – If it can teach another person the use of “I”, then it is an explanation.

### <span class="fac">[185\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/185.webp),[186\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/186.webp)</span>

One can always answer a philosophical question by saying: “As it is now formulated, it is unsolvable. – We must see how it could be formulated in such a way as to allow for an answer. If it is formulated in that way, its answer will present _no_ difficulty.”

### <span class="fac">[186\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/186.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> There is not _one_ method of philosophy, but there are methods, much like different therapies.

### <span class="fac">[186\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/186.webp)</span>

“I am in pain” relates to “He is in pain” in a similar way as a groan relates to the statement that someone is groaning.

### <span class="fac">[186\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/186.webp),[187\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/187.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> “I am thinking about my pain.” – How does one do that? I think, for example: “it will soon be over.” But isn’t there still some kind of inner pointing going on here, by which I connect “it” with the pain? – “You certainly can’t deny that I _mean_ something with this word in my inner self!” – I don’t want to deny anything; for example, not that when you say “it,” you make a certain movement, you direct your attention away from certain things, etc.; but in what way does this help the _thought_? _What_ connection exists between the function of the expression of the thought & the focusing of attention on the pain when uttering the word “it”?

### <span class="fac">[187\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/187.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Suppose you have pain & at the same time hear someone playing the piano next door. You say: “it will soon stop.” Surely, there is a difference whether you mean the pain or the piano playing! – Of course; but in what does this difference consist? I admit: in many cases, the opinion will correspond to a direction of attention, just as often a glance, a gesture, or a closing of the eyes, which one might call ‘looking inward,’ does.

### <span class="fac">[187\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/187.webp),[188\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/188.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> One says, “I have only been thinking about my pain all the time,” when one has directed one’s attention to nothing else.

### <span class="fac">[188\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/188.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Suppose someone simulates pain & now says: “it will soon stop” – can one not say of him that he means the pain, & yet he is not concentrating his attention on any pain? – And what if I finally say: “it has already stopped”?

### <span class="fac">[188\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/188.webp),[189\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/189.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> (But can one also lie by saying: “it will soon stop” & meaning the pain & answering the question “what did you mean?” with: “the noise in the next room”? In cases of this kind, one says something like: “I wanted to say: …, but I changed my mind & said: …”.

### <span class="fac">[189\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/189.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> One can speak _of_ an object by pointing to it while speaking. The pointing is here part of the language-game. Now one believes that one is thereby speaking _of_ a sensation by directing one’s attention to it while speaking. But where is the analogy? It clearly lies in the fact that looking & listening can serve as a kind of pointing. But pointing _to_ the object that one speaks of can also be quite immaterial for the language-game, for the thought.

### <span class="fac">[189\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/189.webp),[190\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/190.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> And what, then, do I point to through the inner activity of listening? To the sound that reaches my ears & to the silence when I hear _nothing_? Listening _seeks_, as it were, an auditory impression & can therefore not point to it, but only to the _place_ where it seeks it.

### <span class="fac">[190\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/190.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> If the receptive attitude is called ‘pointing’ (to something), then not to what we gain through this attitude.

### <span class="fac">[190\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/190.webp),[191\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/191.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Suppose I listened attentively to a sound that is growing ever fainter. I finally say: “it will soon stop altogether.” In what sense did I mean the sound by “it”? And how would I have to go about it to mean something else in this situation? – What business is this ‘meaning’ of mine? Just think of the practical use of thought, of language! What meaning could the question have as to whether I already meant this or that with this word, or not?? I say: “It will soon stop”—well, why am I making this noise at all? What good does it do? What is its significance? Would this sequence of sounds have any significance for us if, without being a part of a _language_, it had been uttered in this _one_ instance, whatever the psychological context might be?

### <span class="fac">[191\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/191.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> When I say in the description of an experiment: this and that must take place _for half an hour_, it must be known by what standard the passage of half an hour is to be judged. And when I say that it belongs to a thought that this mental attitude accompanies the word …, it must be known how that is to be judged. The mental attitude does not ‘_accompany_’ the word in the same sense that a gesture accompanies it. (Similarly, just as one can travel _alone_ & yet be accompanied by my wishes, & just as a room can be empty & yet be filled with light.)

### <span class="fac">[192\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/192.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Suppose you telephone someone and say to him: “this table is too high,” pointing with your finger at the table – what role does this pointing play here? Can I say: I _mean_ the table in question by pointing at it? What is the point of this pointing, and what is the point of these words and whatever else may accompany them?

### <span class="fac">[192\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/192.webp)</span>

“Meaning,” one might say, is not a verb.

### <span class="fac">[192\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/192.webp),[193\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/193.webp)</span>

What kind of connection should there be between word and sensation, if one considers the inner focusing on the sensation; what is the purpose of this connection? Was I taught _that_ when I learned to use this sentence, to think this thought? (For the thought is something that I learned – a move in a game.) We also learn, of course, to direct our attention to things or to sensations: we learn to _observe_ and to describe the observation. But how is my ‘inner activity’ controlled in this case? According to what is it judged whether I have really paid attention?

### <span class="fac">[193\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Does one say, for example: “I didn’t really mean my pain now, I didn’t pay enough attention to it”? Do I ask myself: “What did I mean with this word now? My attention was divided between my pain and the noise – –.”

### <span class="fac">[193\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/193.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The directing of attention does not intervene in the mechanism of thoughts _in the way_ one is tempted to assume.

### <span class="fac">[193\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/193.webp),[194\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/194.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> When I ask: “What do you mean by…?”, I am asking for a _specific connection_. But not every connection interests me, and, for example, generally not the one that consists in the speaker imagining this and that while uttering the word.

### <span class="fac">[194\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/194.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Why should one call this word-expression accompanied by this mental act the thought? Have we learned to call _this_ the thought? Have we learned to use the statement “I mean…” _in this way_? Or do we rather only _subsequently_ interpret the verb as a designation for this act?

### <span class="fac">[194\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/194.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> With the answer: “I meant… …,” (he) establishes a connection, and does _not_ report, on the basis of introspection, that this and that connection exists.

### <span class="fac">[194\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/194.webp),[195\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/195.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> I meant my pain = I said it with regard to my pain. And yet we _do not_ use “mean” to perform a designation of such a mental act. The meaning of the word “mean” virtually bypasses this act. When one _means_ this and that, one does certain things. But the verb “mean” does not mean: to do this.

### <span class="fac">[195\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/195.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Compare “to mean” with the French expression “vouloir dire.” This might lead us to think about how one _might want_ to say one thing while saying something else.

### <span class="fac">[195\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/195.webp),[196\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/196.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “It will stop soon.” – You’re not going to tell me that whoever says that doesn’t already mean what they mean! – Can someone who doesn’t know German, through the accompanying mental processes, mean this sequence of sounds in that way? Or think that it isn’t even a sentence in a language! –

### <span class="fac">[196\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/196.webp),[197\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/197.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> I listen to a sound & say: “What a wonderful melody!” The other person: “I don’t hear a melody.” – I: “I don’t mean what we’re hearing here, but the song …” (which we had talked about earlier, for example). This only shows, one might say, that _“to mean”_ is not the same as _listening_—but rather a different, specific process. – So whoever says: “I meant the song…” is telling the other person that the particular process called “meaning” (& which, admittedly, no one seems to know all that well) took place in him (the speaker) with regard to this song. But why is he telling him this? Is it of interest to the other person? – I wouldn’t ask that if they had been conducting a psychological experiment. – You also don’t go on to talk about the process of “meaning” but rather about that melody. If my words “I meant …” were a communication about this rather unclear mental process, it seems in any case to have been quite irrelevant to the conversation. As irrelevant, for example, as the statement in a chess game that I just had the peculiar sensation of threatening the opponent’s king.

### <span class="fac">[197\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/197.webp),[198\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/198.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The truth is: if someone wants to give me a communication about a particular experience with the words “I meant the song…,” I will have to answer him: “I don’t yet know what you mean. Describe your experience more precisely: I can still imagine all sorts of things.” This is clearly not the use we usually make of the word “mean.”

### <span class="fac">[198\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/198.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Introspection could at least lead us to see that there are, in any case, several varieties of mental processes that one could call ‘processes of meaning.’ – And then we might well be surprised that the question: “Tell me, _how_ did you mean the song—_this_ way… or _that_ way…?” would seem incomprehensible to us. (Is this case comparable to the following: It may be interesting to have knowledge of what I wrote to so-and-so; but who would ask me, for example, “How did you write the ‘L’ at the beginning of this letter?”!)

### <span class="fac">[198\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/198.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The expression “meaning the pain” does not only mean the same thing as “paying attention to the pain,” but the use of the one is quite different from the use of the other.

### <span class="fac">[198\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/198.webp),[199\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/199.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “It will soon be over.” – – “Did you mean the pain?” – “Yes.” – “Then I will not give you any more pain-relieving powder.” What interests the person who asks: “Did you mean…?”? Not what is going on in the other person while uttering the words “It will…” – Under other circumstances, he could also have asked: “Tell me, what was going on in you when you spoke the word…?” But the answer to that would never be: “I meant…”! Rather, it would be much less simple & much less clear.

### <span class="fac">[199\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/199.webp),[200\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Suppose two people are conducting an experiment; one has to observe a column of mercury; he says to the other: “it is rising,” or “it is still rising,” or “now it is falling”; he means the mercury by this. How does this work? He observes & speaks. – Suppose he says “it is rising” again, & this time he means something else – for example, his consciousness. How can he begin to do this? That is, what would we call “meaning his consciousness” under such circumstances? – Something like this: He said to himself: “My consciousness had decreased considerably, but now it is rising,” & he uttered the last words aloud.

### <span class="fac">[200\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “Did he mean the column of mercury by observing it?” That he meant it was contained in the whole game that was played.

### <span class="fac">[200\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Concentrating attention on my sensation corresponds, in the language-game, to observing, not to pointing.

### <span class="fac">[200\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/200.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> One could also ask: Does the sentence, “The noise is getting quieter,” have a continuously changing sense because the noise is changing? – And this shows that it is not immediately clear _what_ I am ‘internally pointing to’ with these words.

### <span class="fac">[200\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/200.webp),[201\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/201.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> “What was going on when you meant that word…?” “What was going on when ‘z’ meant the tangent of α in this calculation?”

### <span class="fac">[201\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/201.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> On the other hand: “When you cursed just now, did you really mean it?” means something like: “Were you really angry at the time?” And the answer can be given on the basis of introspection & is often of the kind: “I didn’t mean it _very_ seriously,” “I meant it half in jest,” etc.; there are degrees here. And one also says: “I was half thinking of _him_ when I said that word.”

### <span class="fac">[201\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/201.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “I mean this word…” one would like to compare with “This word brings something to my mind.” But this gives the word “mean” a false grammatical interpretation.

### <span class="fac">[201\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/201.webp),[202\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/202.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Examine this case: “When you said just now, ‘it will soon be over’ – did you mean your pain, or the noise?” – “I don’t remember what I meant.”

### <span class="fac">[202\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/202.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Does he learn the use of “I have…” by being told: if _this_ person has pain, then you must say “_I_ have…”? – Why not? It only depends on how the words “this person” are to be used, according to which its identity is to be determined.

### <span class="fac">[202\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/202.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Each such basis [replacing one expression with another] is ambiguous.

### <span class="fac">[202\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/202.webp),[203\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/203.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> Someone can say: “_That_ looks red to me now, but yesterday I saw it green,” & under certain circumstances, _e.g._ if we have a physiological explanation for it, we will accept this statement. – But if someone repeatedly made such statements, remembering that he saw it differently yesterday, without anything else speaking for such statements, we would eventually stop using the expression “he remembers…” & use the expression: “he _says_ he remembers…”. And we would perhaps add: “but I don’t know what that actually means.”

If someone then replied: “Well, he just has the experiences that we call ‘memory experiences,’” then we would suddenly be inclined to dismiss this as an irrelevant remark; we would no longer know what to do with the idea of an inner experience, & we would be inclined to let it drop. It suddenly becomes pointless to speak of ‘a particular experience’.

### <span class="fac">[203\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/203.webp),[204\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/204.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Let us take an experience that you would call a “memory experience” – in order to bring to mind what you call “memory,” you might recall an event from the previous day. Yes, now you know what “memory experience” means. But do you know it for certain – is it not possible that tomorrow, when you try to bring to mind what is meant by this word, a completely different inner experience might occur to you? You will then consider this experience and describe what it consists of. You will see that there are various ‘memory experiences’ and that their description will consist of giving the words that one is inclined to say in this case, the gestures, facial expressions that one makes, the picture that comes to mind. – Now take any such experience and ask yourself whether you would call it a “memory experience” under _all_ circumstances. I remember seeing N. in his room; I see him before me, sitting at his desk: is this memory-image of a different kind than any image of my imagination? Could the sequences of sounds: “I remember…” mean something completely different in another language?

### <span class="fac">[204\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/204.webp),[205\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/205.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Would we call it a language-game if that person, when looking at the signs in his diary, drew any drawings he pleased? – But would that not constitute a mental experience? And why should _that_ not be language, but what is associated with another kind of experience should be? If that drawing were now memory-related (whatever that may mean), how would it become more important to us?

### <span class="fac">[205\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/205.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The dentist asks: “Do you have pain here?” The patient: “I _don’t_ have anything – Ouch, it hurts!”

### <span class="fac">[205\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/205.webp),[206\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/206.webp),[207\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/207.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “There is a subjective regularity, a regularity that exists only for _me_.” – That is, we sometimes use the word “regularity” in such a way: someone imagines a regularity; he sees something regularly; something seems regular to him, etc. But that does not mean that he has an object in front of him – which none of us know – and calls it “regularity.” If, besides the game that I see, he is playing another game with himself, of which I know _nothing_, then I also do not know whether it is a ‘game’ to call it so. If he speaks a private language with himself, of which I know _nothing_, besides the public language, why do I say it is a language? That is, we use the _image_ of a ‘private object,’ which only he and no other can see. It is an image – be clear about that! And now it is in the nature of this image that we make further assumptions about this object, and what he does with it; it is not enough for us to say: he has a private something and does something with it. He describes a dream to us, for example, and we say: he sees a picture _before him_ and describes it to us. But then we mean by the ‘picture’ what we usually mean when several people can see the picture. But perhaps you say: “No, I do not mean a material picture, but only the visual impression of such a picture.” – But then it would again be pointless to use the expression “picture, which only he can see” instead of this expression. The mental image is the picture that is described when one describes the imagination. No, we use the simile (image) of a ‘material’ picture, something that we could reproduce according to its description.

### <span class="fac">[207\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/207.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Of course, if the water in the pot boils, then the steam rises from the pot, and the image of the steam also rises from the image of the pot. But how if one wanted to say that something must also be boiling in the image of the pot?

### <span class="fac">[207\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/207.webp),[208\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/208.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Now, if we say: “of course, we don’t actually know what his image looks like,” then we thereby nullify our assumption, and one can ask: if you don’t know _what_ he has before him, on what grounds do you call it an “image”? If, for example, it were a surface with (completely) arbitrary patches of color, would you still be justified in saying that he _describes_ this complex with his words? If you say that he has a private image before him, then you must now work from the outside _in_. (That is,) you must assume something inside _for the explanation_ – _more accurately_, _for the representation_ – _of the outside_; if you do not do this, then your assumption loses _all_ sense. Of course, you don’t see it as an assumption; it seems to us that _language_ says it. “He has a private object. Furthermore, we know nothing about it.” – But what do we know if we know _that_? –

### <span class="fac">[208\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/208.webp),[209\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/209.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The way our language expresses itself suggests the image, or makes use of the image, of an object that belongs to me alone. – Consider that one can make the same transition from one image of language to another _for different purposes_. (For example, one wants to move from _one_ hypothesis to another; or one wants to express the same hypothesis differently.)

### <span class="fac">[209\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/209.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> What would one say against the idea that the “u” is a double i (‘the dots are omitted’), and that this double i is pronounced differently from the simple one? Does one actually say something about the “u” when one says that it is a double i, without (thereby) wanting to make any claims about the history of the letter “u,” or its pronunciation?

### <span class="fac">[209\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/209.webp),[210\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/210.webp)</span>
