<span class="series-number">2</span> If you say that he has a private image before him that he describes, then you have at least made an assumption about what he has before him. And that means that you can describe it more precisely, or are describing it. If you admit that you have no idea what kind of thing he might have before him, what then still leads you to say that he has something before him? Isn’t that the same as saying of someone: He _has_ something – but whether it is money, or a debt, or an empty cash box, I do not know.

### <span class="fac">[210\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/210.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Isn’t it the case that you first form a specific image of what he has before him, then declare it to be unfounded, but nevertheless (persist in the idea) that he has something before him?

### <span class="fac">[210\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/210.webp),[211\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/211.webp),[212\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/212.webp),[213\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/213.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Where does the idea come from that someone has a private image before them? – “Well, because I have one!” – But do you have an _image_ before you? You’re just saying that! And there is as little & as much reason to use this phrase in your case as in that of the other person. “But I do, for example, vividly imagine something, so I see it before me, and the other person standing next to me does not see it, so it belongs to me alone.” – But it is (only) a metaphor to say that you see something “in front of you” or “within you”—the simile of the ‘inner eye.’ And if the other person imagines the same thing, why shouldn’t you say that he has _the same thing_ in front of him? – “But he can’t possibly have the exact same pain that I have!” – Why not? What is the criterion of identity? And how is it: Can he not have it at the same time as you, but can have it once you have put it aside; or can he never have _it_ at all? (Strange, the ideas we come up with!)

“But when I imagine something, or even see real objects, I still _have_ something my neighbor does not have.” – I have understanding for you. You want to look around and say: “Only _I_ have _this_.” – But what’s the point of these words? They’re _useless_. – Yes, couldn’t you also say: “We’re not talking here about ‘_seeing_’ (and therefore also about ‘having’) and about a subject, that is, about the ‘I’”? Couldn’t I say to you: What you’re talking about and saying only you have—in what sense _do_ you _have_ it? Do you possess it? You don’t even _see_ it. Yes, shouldn’t you say of it: no one _has_ it? It’s clear, after all: if you _logically_ rule out that another has something, then it also loses its sense to say that _you_ have it. But what, then, is this thing you’re talking about? I did say: I know within myself what you’re talking about. But that didn’t mean I could point to the object you were speaking of; yet I know how to grasp this object, to see how you intended to designate it, so to speak, through your gaze and gestures. I know in what way one looks in front of and around oneself in this case, and other things. – I believe one can say: You speak (when you are sitting in the room, for example) of the ‘visual room’. That which has no owner is the ‘visual room’. I can no more possess it than I can walk around in it, or look at it, or point to it. It does not belong to me insofar as it cannot belong to anyone else; or: it does not belong to me insofar as I (indeed) want to use the same form of expression for it as (e.g.) for the material room itself in which I am sitting. This can, after all, be described without there being any question of owning the room. And it is quite possible, after all, that the room has no owner. But then the visual room _cannot_ have an owner either. “For it has no master except itself & none within itself”—one might say. Just imagine a landscape, an imaginary landscape, and within it a house—and someone asked: “Who owns the house?” (The answer, by the way, could be: “The farmer sitting on the bench in front of the front door.”—But this farmer cannot, for example, enter his house.)

### <span class="fac">[213\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/213.webp),[214\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/214.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> – “I do say: ‘I now have this and that idea,’ but the words ‘I have’ are merely a sign for the _other_; the world of the imagination is _entirely_ represented in the description of the idea.” – You mean, “I have” is more like: “Now pay attention!” You are inclined to say that it should actually be expressed differently. Perhaps simply by making a gesture with the hand and then describing it. – When, as here, one disagrees with the expressions of our ordinary language (which do, after all, serve their purpose), an image sits in our mind that conflicts with that of the ordinary mode of expression. While we are tempted to say that our way of expressing things does not describe the facts as they really are. – As if (for example) the sentence “he is in pain” could be wrong in any other way than by the fact that this person is _not_ in pain. So one wants to say, for instance: “he _is_ not actually in pain”—without thereby meaning to say that he is not in pain. As if the form of expression were saying something false, even though the sentence, if necessary, asserts something true. For _this_ is indeed _how_ the disputes between idealists, solipsists, and realists appear. The former attack ordinary language as if they were attacking an assertion; the latter defend it as if they were stating obvious facts that every reasonable person acknowledges.

### <span class="fac">[215\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/215.webp)</span>

It is correct, although paradoxical, to say: “‘I’ does not refer to a person.”

### <span class="fac">[215\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/215.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> If I want to describe the use of the word “I”—is it sufficient for me to describe how persons A, B, C, etc., use the word—or must I also say how _I_ use it? So will the word “I” also appear in _this_ way in the description of the language-game? – “Yes, are you saying that you, L.W., have a special use for this word?” – No; but I didn’t want to say “I, L.W.” either, just “I.” ‒ ‒ This is the trick this word can play on us.

### <span class="fac">[215\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/215.webp),[216\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/216.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “When I am in pain, there is simply _pain_, & there is no mention of a person at all.” – So you simply want to say: “Pain!” – this _describes_ – you would say – the whole fact. But, first of all, is that a description? & secondly, what is it good for? You _are_ evidently _conducting a comparison of_ the situation with one in which you have to give a description. The idea is a world that is to be described; like (for example) the Earth as described in a geography book. The description could also be spoken of in terms of a gramophone. – But in what sense is the expression of pain a description of a world; _& what use is this description?_

### <span class="fac">[216\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/216.webp),[217\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Imagine two descriptions. In one, it says: at such-and-such a place on Earth stands a house that looks like this and that, is furnished like this, etc. In the other: “Once upon a time there was a rich man who lived in a house …” (now the description follows). Or I say: “Let’s imagine a house that …”. The purpose of each of these descriptions is different. (What if someone were to say: the description of the house is actually all that matters; the rest is merely a hint for the listener?) It is a false comparison that misleads us.

### <span class="fac">[217\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Or rather, a hundred misleading comparisons seem to converge here: one takes for an ostensive explanation what is not one; & for a description what is not a description; & for a proper name what is not one; & for knowledge what is not knowledge.

### <span class="fac">[217\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/217.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> One must proceed cautiously, as if on thin ice; one must ask about usage everywhere, and trust the appearance of the expression nowhere. For each of the common expressions suggests a usage other than the fact.

### <span class="fac">[217\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/217.webp),[218\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/218.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> A hundred misleading images converge here, and _that_ is what constitutes the difficulty of the philosophical situation. Wherever we step, the ground gives way again. The ‘great,’ difficult problems of philosophy do not arise because there is some incredibly subtle and mysterious state of affairs here that we are to investigate, but because a great multitude of misleading forms of expression intersect at _this_ point.

### <span class="fac">[218\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/218.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Why did I say that the “I have” is only for the other? – That means: not for me, who describes the image. And that is supposed to mean: I don’t need this hint – so presumably: I know that it is _my_ image. – – I take it for granted that this ‘description’ (in some way) means something to me. In what way is it not merely a noise accompanying an image? That is: what can _I_ do with it?

### <span class="fac">[218\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/218.webp),[219\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/219.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “What more can he know than how things are? And he learns this through the description of the image.” Here, this description is understood as the description of a universe, a world. In fact, if he receives a description, he can still do very different things with it. The “I have” can (for example) be compared with an orientation arrow on a map. And that also belongs to the map. (Although one could say: not to the map, as _image_.)

### <span class="fac">[219\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/219.webp),[220\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> It is probably true that he receives a complete description with the description of my visual image – because the “I have” does not add anything to the _description_. But we want to say: “What _more_ can he have than a complete description!” – & we forget that what we call “description” can occupy different places in the language-game. We are not surprised that the description of a landscape has different application possibilities if we add a hint to it: this landscape is located here & there; one can get there in this way. – But the description of the visual image is in a different case, because it describes – we would like to say – a world; & that is because the visual image is not limited to anything.

### <span class="fac">[220\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> We cannot arrive at a solution to the philosophical difficulty until we remove, as it were, the blood from the sentences that hold us captive; by directing our attention to where we got this form of representation from; that is, to what served as a model for it.

### <span class="fac">[220\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/220.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Coming up with a new possibility of how something could have happened is also a _discovery_. And that is why one often thinks that one has discovered the actual state of affairs when one has only found a new possibility of how it could have been. (Darwin’s theory.)

### <span class="fac">[221\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> You think that you must weave a dress: because you are sitting in front of (albeit an empty) loom & making the movements of weaving.

### <span class="fac">[221\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> One could also say that the owner of the visual room must be essentially the same as it; but he is not in it, nor is there an outside.

### <span class="fac">[221\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> “The visual room has no owner” means as much as: it has no neighbor.

### <span class="fac">[221\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/221.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> What the one who seemed to have _discovered_ the ‘visual room’—what he had found—was a new form of expression, a new comparison; & one could also say, a new sensation.

### <span class="fac">[222\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/222.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Imagine someone who, while looking at the sun, suddenly has the _sensation_ that it is not moving – that _we_ are passing by it. Now he wants to say that he has seen a new state of motion in which we are; & he thinks that he is now making gestures to show which movement he means, & that it is not that of the sun. – Here we would have to do with two different applications of the word “movement”.

### <span class="fac">[222\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/222.webp),[223\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/223.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> You interpret the new conception as seeing a new object. You interpret a grammatical move that you make as a quasi-physical phenomenon that you discover. (Think, for example, of the question: “Are sense-data the building blocks of the universe?”) But it is not entirely correct to express oneself in this way: you have made a ‘grammatical’ move. You have primarily discovered a new conception. It is as if you had invented a new style of painting; or also, a new meter, or a new type of song. –

### <span class="fac">[223\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/223.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> One can possess a mirror; does that mean one also possesses the reflection that appears in it?

### <span class="fac">[223\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/223.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “One should actually say: …” – Why should one _actually_ use a different expression if this one also does the job? Because the image contained in our usual expression does not (now) fit us; because, for whatever reason, we are striving for a different conception; but not because the other expression now describes the fact (more correctly) or more accurately.

### <span class="fac">[223\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/223.webp),[224\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/224.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> “Sentences are there to describe how everything is related,” we think. The sentence as a _picture_. And that is quite nice, but there are also still lifes, portraits, landscape paintings, mythological depictions, ornaments, maps, diagrams, etc., etc.

### <span class="fac">[224\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/224.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> To play a game of chess in one’s head. – To play a game of tennis in one’s head. – To give oneself a present for one’s birthday. – To buy a house from oneself.

### <span class="fac">[224\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/224.webp),[225\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/225.webp),[226\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/226.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> How do we go about seeing the ‘visual room’; I mean that which is a world & belongs to no subject? For that is precisely why we think we _have discovered_ it, because we are not always conscious of it. – On the one hand, of course, I see everything there just as usual; I look around, including at my body, _and think_: “I cannot see my eyes”— – But there are surely all sorts of _ways_ to see my surroundings like this. I can, for example, ‘imagine’ that all the objects I see are made of painted paper & physically exist only on the side that is now facing me. And what does it consist of, to see things this way and that way? – Sometimes partly in letting the gaze rest or wander, and in how it wanders; or in being entirely _an eye_, and in what we say—and _do not_ say—in the process; in the gestures we make; and in many other things. We receive some impressions only when we stare at a spot; some only when all objects around us are at rest, not when something is moving; some probably only indoors, when all distances are small; & if people always philosophized outdoors, they would not come up with certain thoughts. One might say: when we philosophize, not only our language celebrates, but also our gaze. For while I am stoking the stove, I see it differently than when I stare at it while philosophizing—I do not think of the ‘visual stove,’ the sense-datum, etc. A philosopher who always closes one eye while philosophizing might be more easily captivated by other perspectives than one who always looks with both.

### <span class="fac">[226\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/226.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Whoever says that one cannot step twice into the same river can only feel so under very special circumstances; that is, only under these circumstances does he try to say it.

### <span class="fac">[226\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/226.webp),[227\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/227.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The different roles that a _picture_ can assume in a theory (or in a language-game in general) – the different positions in which an algebraic picture (let’s say <math class="stacked" display="inline"><mo>√</mo><mover><mrow><mtext>x²</mtext><mspace width="0.3em"/><mo>+</mo><mspace width="0.3em"/><mtext>y²</mtext><mspace width="0.3em"/><mo>+</mo><mspace width="0.3em"/><mtext>z²</mtext></mrow><mo>‾</mo></mover></math>) can appear in a mathematical expression. I believe that one can illustrate the former by means of the latter. Whoever probably knows that this root plays a role in this expression, but not whether it is the base or the exponent, whether it is above or below the fraction line, actually doesn’t know very much yet.

### <span class="fac">[227\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/227.webp),[228\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/228.webp),[229\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/229.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The discussion about the diary writer. The odd thing is that it doesn’t seem entirely clear what is meant when one says that he sees, has, mental images when he rereads his diary. Where does this come from? It seems as if I am making a mistake when I speak so readily about him having (or _perhaps_ having) mental images. The first thing that occurs to me is that I cannot simply assume that a table has mental images. And if someone said: “Yes, because you don’t assume that the table has a mind,” I ask: _why_ can I not assume that? _What_ prevents me from doing so? It must be shape & behaviour. That is to say: the _actions_ are probably the criterion for whether something ‘has a mind’. If I said: “Imagine that this table is now looking at a picture,” you wouldn’t really know how to apply a ‘_picture_’ to the table. But why does it work when you have a person in front of you instead? For example, you don’t primarily look at his feet or his stomach when you want to imagine him looking at a picture; rather, you look at his head. Say what you want – you mean that the idea is in his head. And one can see that it is there from his facial expression – also posture, etc.. This is the _picture_ that you use; but what is its application? It is indeed true: I cannot imagine how a table looks at a picture (one could also say: ‘with which _part_’) – but I can imagine how a person does this. And one will also say against any objection: “I can think that this person is looking at a picture!” That is to say, above all: You can say it, & it is a well-established German sentence with which you – usually – probably know how to deal. But we also usually don’t feel any difficulty in saying: “I assume that he is imagining …”. Because we don’t even get to the point of asking ourselves how a state of mind, how an image, is somehow _attached_ to the person, but rather we _work_ with our expression. (We take in money, spend it again – but we don’t stare at a banknote & try to see the _value_ that is somehow attached to it.) And not because we are then unthinking – but because we _think_ – but without having a mental cramp.

### <span class="fac">[229\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/229.webp),[230\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> So if I feel that I don’t quite know what I mean: that man sees pictures in front of him – although I would like to say that I very well understand what that means – it is because I don’t quite know how to apply my statement. And therefore the feeling, as if I didn’t know how the images are _attached_ to this man. They are, in fact, attached to what someone says & does (to his actions).

### <span class="fac">[230\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/230.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The cause is often the lightning rod that robs facts of their power. One says: “This is because …,” & has derived the power from the fact. (One could often say that one has grounded the fact.)

### <span class="fac">[230\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/230.webp),[231\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/231.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “Time exists everywhere in all of space.” To demonstrate that this is so, I stand somewhere and make a gesture that indicates the passage of time. And I say to the other: “You see, time passes. –” And isn’t it as if I really see, or feel, it? Isn’t that remarkable! What do I perceive? Perhaps a hand movement and whatever else is currently happening, and yet I would like to say that I am now conscious of the passage of time. I am conscious of an image that brings to mind the words “the passage of time.”

### <span class="fac">[231\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/231.webp),[232\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/232.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> When he sees a certain phenomenon in the sky, he makes a cross in his diary. – This is the description of a language-game. Here we imagine certain circumstances; a person whose reliability has been tested in the usual way. Now I disregard these familiar circumstances & consider only the subjective language-game. He now makes a cross in the diary when he ‘thinks he sees’ those phenomena. And _I accept_ this; for I have no further indication that he thinks he sees the phenomenon. But then I assume that something is happening there where no one is looking.

### <span class="fac">[232\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/232.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “Blind is the one who does not see; however one may determine this & whether anyone knows it or not.” But that is not how the word “blind” is used! Of course, blind is the one who does not see—i.e., the expressions mean the same thing; they are used in the _same_ way. With the explanation: “‘blind’ is the one who does not see,” one now, as it were, provides a picture of blindness; something like this:

![](../graphics/116,232-1.svg)

as opposed to this:

![](../graphics/116,232-2.svg)

But that doesn’t mean we’ve gotten rid of the criteria for being blind. These criteria still determine the use of the word “blind.”

### <span class="fac">[233\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/233.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “I know what it is to feel pain.” Well, what is it? Can you continue: “It is like _this_” by assuring me that you felt something at the last word? This means as little as saying: “I know what the color ‘cobalt blue’ is: _this_,” if you do not point to anything when using the demonstrative pronoun. “But does it mean nothing to the one who says it?” – What does he do with that? –

### <span class="fac">[233\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/233.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> And why should one not say: “One can never _know_ that someone does not see; our observations can only make it highly probable”? Why should one not also use this form of expression, however complicated and misleading it may be?

### <span class="fac">[233\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/233.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> How does the assumption of a state of affairs turn into the assumption of a form of expression? The image of the differential.

### <span class="fac">[234\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/234.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> If one now says: “I can _assume_ that this person has this and that image” – then the answer is: You can in any case say: “I assume…,” and you can also imagine something (think) while doing so, and that is probably all that is involved in making an assumption; but does this also determine the role that this assumption plays? It can still (to put it that way) be a _dead_ assumption.

### <span class="fac">[234\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/234.webp),[235\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “The assumption that this person – who behaves quite normally – is blind, makes sense!” – That is: ‘it is an assumption,’ ‘I can really make such an assumption.’ And this means (doesn’t it): I really form a picture of what I am assuming. Yes; but does it go any further? If I make the assumption that someone is blind under other circumstances, I never confirm that this assumption really makes sense. And that I really think something, have an image, then plays no role at all. This image only becomes important here, where it is, as it were, the only indication that I have really made an assumption. Yes, it is all that is left of an assumption here.

### <span class="fac">[235\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> If I hold on to an assumption, the question is precisely _what_ I am holding on to with this assumption.

### <span class="fac">[235\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/235.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “But you say: ‘I have pain,’ because _you_ have pain.” – I.e.: Your “I have…” is justified by _this_ phenomenon. – Yes; just think how the expressive term “this phenomenon” is used in this case.

### <span class="fac">[235\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/235.webp),[236\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/236.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> “Nothing in the visual field points to this, etc.” (Log. Phil. Abh.) This means, so to speak: You will search in vain within the visual field for the act of _seeing_. It is nowhere to be found within the visual field. – But the truth is: You _are_ only _pretending_ to search for something, for a person within the visual field who is not there.

### <span class="fac">[236\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/236.webp),[237\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/237.webp),[238\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/238.webp),[239\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/239.webp),[240\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/240.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “In the visual space, no light rays emanate from an object to an eye.” – When I say this, I practically have a picture of this fact! And I have _one_ picture of the visual space, another of the physical space. But the pictures are those of two different spaces. In one, the empty space is as if permeated by lines of construction (of all kinds); in the other, it is empty in the strict sense – as if dark. (And these words themselves do not so much _describe_ the two pictures, but belong to these pictures themselves.) Now, remember that in our sentence we said something about _the ‘nature’_ of the visual space; but that with it we have not yet made any _practical use_ of the expression “the visual space”. How do we want to apply the expression now? Presumably in the communication of subjective visual impression: e.g., in a psychological experiment. We say, for example: “in my visual space, objects are in the following arrangement: …”. And instead of “in my visual space”, one can simply say “_in the visual space_”, and the possessive pronoun is eliminated by the special practice of the application of the expression. It is easy to think of the rules of such an application. – And whoever finds this kind of representation (for whatever reasons) appealing will be inclined to say: There is no ‘_my_’ & ‘_his_’ visual space; there is only _the_ visual space. Let us think of the description of a picture. Let it be a landscape; two forms of description are possible. In one, it says: The evening sun illuminates the peaks of the mountains … the trees cast long shadows … the clouds are reflected in the lake, etc. In the other: The sun is just above the horizon … the peaks of the mountains are bright … the trees have long shadows … in the lake one sees blue & white clouds, etc. (Perhaps one will say that the first type of description is only applicable where the lights & shadows, etc. are actually motivated in the picture. But this is not the case. If, for example, there were a completely unmotivated brightness in one place in the picture, we can simply say: “from an invisible source, bright light falls on …”. ) Now, if someone said: “In the space of a picture, no light falls from one object on another” – what could he want with this statement? Is it not a (special) way of looking at things that he highlights with it? The sentence is timeless; I do not want to say: “in the picture space, light _never_ falls …”; not: experience teaches …; but: it is in the nature of the picture space. But one could also use the sentence like this: “It is useless that you paint the sun in this picture even brighter, the mountains will not become brighter because of it.” The way of looking at things that has been presented to us is approximately this: Even in the picture there _are_ a right & left, a front & back, & _spatial objects_; they are bright here, dark here; but there are no (well-known) causal connections between these brightnesses & darknesses. – _One_ analogy is thus highlighted, another suppressed. The expression “in the picture space, no light falls, etc.” draws us in another direction, (puts us on a different line of thought). We imagine a physical space in which the objects have a (so to speak) magical brightness, but do not act on each other in the usual way through their brightness. Now, if someone says: “In the visual space, no light rays go …” – I do not yet know for sure how he wants to use this statement. He could, for example, continue: “I want to say that not in all cases in which something _is seen_, it is seen with the eye.” But I can best explain the sentence like this: –If I say: “in the visual space, rays go from there to there”, this means that _luminous lines_ pass through the space; where these cannot be seen, where (as one can also say) these are not present in the visual space, one does not speak of ‘rays in the visual space’. I want to show how easily one can, through natural transitions from one form of representation to another, arrive at a sentence that has the character of a statement about a foreign world; & which, however, only presents a foreign picture to us for the representation of well-known things.

### <span class="fac">[240\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/240.webp),[241\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/241.webp),[242\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/242.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Where do I see the house: here in my eye, or there, where it stands? (In philosophy, one keeps forgetting that one has two eyes.) But suppose I decide on one of the two answers; what consequence would that decision have? – Task: One says: “I see a house there”; how is this sentence applied? And how could one apply: “I see the house _here_” (while pointing to one eye, or to both eyes)? – Compare this with: “When I feel this object with a stick, I have the sensation of touch at the tip of the stick, not in the hand holding it.” When someone says: “I do not feel this pressure here in my hand, but in my wrist”—the consequence is that the doctor examines the wrist. But what difference does it make whether I say I feel the hardness of the object at the tip of the stick, or in my hand? Does what I say mean: “It is as if I had nerve endings at the tip of the stick”? _In what sense_ is that so?—Well, in any case, I am inclined to say: “I feel the hardness, etc., at the tip of the stick”; and this goes hand in hand with the fact that when palpating, I see not my hand but the tip of the stick, that I describe what I feel with the words: “I feel something hard and round there”—not by saying: “I feel pressure against the fingertips of my thumb, middle finger, and index finger.” If, for example, someone asked me: “What do you feel now in the fingers holding the probe?” – (then) I could answer him: “I don’t know – – I feel something hard and rough _there_.”

### <span class="fac">[242\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/242.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> When I recite the ABC in my mind, what is the criterion for knowing that I am doing the same thing as someone else who is reciting it silently? It might be found that the same process is taking place in my larynx and his. (And likewise, when we both think the same thing, wish the same thing, etc.) But do we learn the usage of the words “recite this and that silently” by referring to a process in the larynx or in the brain? Is it not also quite possible that different physiological processes correspond to my conception of the sound _a_ and his? The question is: _How does_ one _perform a comparison_ of conceptions?

### <span class="fac">[242\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/242.webp),[243\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/243.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> ‘I can never _know_ that he is reciting _it_ to himself in his mind.’ – But does he know it himself? What if we said: He cannot know it either; he can only _say_ it? He can—one might say—just as little as I _can verify_ that what he imagines is really what we call the sound _a_. – ‘So he doesn’t know whether he is imagining the sound _a_?!’ – No, to say _that_ would be wrong. It would imply that he is uncertain, that he is in doubt about it. It is more correct to say that neither knowledge _nor_ doubt is at issue here. – The _language-game_ begins with him _saying_ that he has the idea …

### <span class="fac">[243\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/243.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> The great difficulty here is not to present the matter as if one were incapable of doing something: namely, as if there were indeed an object from which I derive the description, but I were unable to show it to anyone. – And the best I can suggest is probably that we give in to the temptation to use this image: but now examine how it can be applied further.

### <span class="fac">[244\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/244.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> The conception of memory as an internal process makes it possible for us to make an assumption about the process, seemingly without concerning ourselves with how and whether the internal process expresses itself. However, the assumption is _empty_ as long as it is not coupled with an assumption about the external processes.

### <span class="fac">[244\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/244.webp),[245\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/245.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Oh, how difficult it is here to step from metaphysics into grammar. – That is, despite all the imagery of the expressions that impose themselves on us, not to forget their _application_.

### <span class="fac">[245\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/245.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> For that is the point: everything ethereal must be left out of the explanation.

### <span class="fac">[245\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/245.webp),[246\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/246.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> It is as if we had inherited a linguistic form that represents things as the products of pouring a material into a form & in which, for example, one speaks of a table in no other way than by saying that the material of the table has taken on the form of a table, so that in the end one believes one must not simply speak of a table, but rather that there is first the material, & then the form into which it enters. The result of philosophy here would be: that one speaks of the table outright & regards this as a form of expression complete in itself.

### <span class="fac">[246\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/246.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Why should I deny that a mental process exists?! Just say, “the mental process of remembering … has now taken place within me”—that is: “I have now remembered …”. To deny the mental process would be to deny the memory; to deny that anyone remembers anything.

### <span class="fac">[246\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/246.webp),[247\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/247.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> If I now say: “the language-game begins with the _utterance_,” is this a grammatical truism? – Let us conduct a comparison between this and: “The language-game begins with the _dream narrative_.” That does not mean, however, that we do not believe someone who says they have dreamed, … – “The language-game begins with the utterance” – well, this is _the difference_: between a description of an ‘inner’ experience and that of a physical fact.

### <span class="fac">[247\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/247.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Since in these investigations we always ask: “_what would_ _one have to say_ if …,” the variety of cases known to us in reality is _not_ sufficient for us; rather, we must take into consideration a multitude of states of affairs, regardless of whether they are real or fictional. That is why it strikes us as odd when we see a philosopher, with the air of a natural scientist, fishing for individual remote facts (strange mental illnesses, for example). As if the factual nature of these things were of importance to us.

### <span class="fac">[247\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/247.webp),[248\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/248.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “I’m saying that I’m in pain because that’s really the case.” – Is that supposed to provide an explanation for the use of the expression “I’m in pain”? How can it—it presupposes it! One might say: – I am using the expression ‘I am in pain’ this time because the reality is that it is _actually_ the case & not because …, e.g., because it is in the script I am learning. What one actually wants to say is: “I am saying that I am in pain because _this_ is the case.” – And if one says this and provides a demonstration that the word “this” does indeed require, – then no one will want to contradict this explanation.

### <span class="fac">[248\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/248.webp),[249\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/249.webp),[250\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/250.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “But where, then, does the notion come from that words are the description of a feeling?” – This is not a notion. It is a delusion that the sentence “Pain is _this_ feeling” is an explanation of the word “pain,” even if it is not accompanied by any _demonstration_. (Related to this: _looking_ can indeed be called a kind of pointing, but not _seeing_.) One would have to speak here of an explanation that can _only_ be laid down in memory. Let us imagine a table that exists only in our imagination. A dictionary, for example. By means of a dictionary, one can justify the translation of a word X into a word Y. But should we also call it a justification if this table is consulted only in the imagination? – “Well, then it is simply a subjective justification.” – But the justification consisted in appealing to an independent source. – “But I can also appeal from my memory of one thing to the memory-image of another. I don’t know—for example—whether I’ve correctly memorized the train’s departure time, so I call up the image of the timetable in my memory to check. Isn’t this the same case here?” – No; for it is essential that this process, based on experience, helps to evoke the _correct_ memory. If the mental image of the timetable could not itself be _checked_ for accuracy, it could not confirm the accuracy of the first memory. (Just as it would make no sense to buy several copies of today’s morning paper to make sure it reports the truth.) Looking up a table in one’s mind is no more an act of looking up a table than the mental image of the result of an imagined experiment is the result of an experiment.

### <span class="fac">[250\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/250.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> It would be almost the same as if, when rolling a die, one determined the value of a roll by rolling it again.

### <span class="fac">[251\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/251.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “I can, after all, resolve (inwardly) to call _this_ ‘pain’ in the future.” – But have you certainly resolved to do so? Are you sure that it was enough to focus your attention on your feeling? – Strange question. –

### <span class="fac">[251\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/251.webp),[252\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/252.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> [The joke of the concept is lost.] Why can’t my right hand give a gift to my left? – Well, it can be done; my right hand can put it into my left. Yes, my right hand could write a deed of gift & my left a receipt, etc. – But the further practical consequences would not be those of a gift. Once the left hand has taken the money from the right, the receipt is written, etc. – one will ask: “Well, & _then_ what?” And the same can be asked when one has given oneself a private explanation of a word.

### <span class="fac">[252\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/252.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “But you can’t deny that an inner process takes place when remembering.” – Why does it give the impression that we want to deny something? When one says, “an inner process does take place,” one wants to continue: “You _can see_ it, after all.” And it is this inner process that is meant by the word “remember.” – It gives the impression that we want to deny something because we are turning against the image of the ‘inner process.’ What I deny is that the image of the ‘inner process’ gives us the correct idea of the use of the word “remember.” Yes, we say that this image, with its ramifications, prevents us from seeing the use of the word as it is.

### <span class="fac">[253\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/253.webp)</span>

### <span class="fac">[253\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/253.webp),[254\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/254.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">4</span> “But surely something was going on inside me when I remembered!” – Why do you say: “_surely_ …”? Is that a _communication_ that something was going on inside you—why the emphasis? I didn’t want to deny it.

### <span class="fac">[254\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/254.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> “What fits A is, in a sense, _the same as_ A!” – Well, I can understand that one is inclined to say that. This statement presupposes that one is familiar with the use of the words “fit” and “be the same.” It underscores an analogy that exists between the use of one word and the other. One could (also) call it an _attempt at persuasion_. It is not to be confused with an assertion of the kind: “For this cylinder to fit into the hollow cylinder, they must have the same diameter when measured with a caliper.” (This is an ‘empirical statement’.)

### <span class="fac">[254\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/254.webp),[255\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> One does not change the grammar of the phrase “the number 3” by calling it ‘an object.’ We thereby agree to say “the object Three” instead of “the number three.” People repeatedly confuse sentences that propose a new image, a new name, with sentences about the nature of an object. If I introduce a new figurative mode of expression, I must now transfer the grammar of the old expression to the new one.

### <span class="fac">[255\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/255.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> “You mean an inner process, don’t you?” – Of course I mean an inner process. Just as I mean a number when I say “3.”

### <span class="fac">[258\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/258.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> Let us imagine a variation of the game of tennis: a rule is added to this game stating that the player must _imagine_ this and that at certain moments of the game, such as when serving. (The purpose of this rule would be to make the game more difficult.) – The first objection might be: it would be too easy to cheat in this game; but this is countered by the assumption that the game is played only by thoroughly honest & reliable people. So here we have a game with internal game actions. –

### <span class="fac">[258\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/258.webp),[259\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/259.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> What kind of inner game action is this, and what does it consist of? Well, in the fact that he—in accordance with the rule of a game—imagines … – But couldn’t one also say: _We do not know_ what kind of inner game action it is that he performs in accordance with the rule; we know only its manifestations? Let the inner game action be an X whose nature we do not know. Or: Perhaps there are only external game actions here as well: the communication of the rule of a game & what is called the ‘manifestation of the internal process.’ – Well, can’t the game be described in all three ways? Even the one involving the ‘unknown’ X is a perfectly possible way of describing it. One says that the so-called ‘internal’ play action is not comparable to a play action in the other sense—another says it _is_ comparable to such an action—a third: it is comparable only to an action that takes place in secret & that no one knows except the actor. What is important for us is that we see the _dangers_ of the expression “inner plot.” It is dangerous because it causes confusion.

### <span class="fac">[261\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/261.webp),[262\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/262.webp)</span>

If I answer, “I meant it”—does that mean the movement was accompanied by certain feelings? What makes the shrug a sign? That we want to communicate something to the other person through it? And is this intention an experience that accompanies the shrug?—But don’t characteristic feelings accompany the shrug as an expression? Oh yes—just as characteristic _facial expressions_ accompany it.

### <span class="fac">[264\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/264.webp)</span>

_Vol. XI, p. 39/3_

### <span class="fac">[265\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> Imagine a language-game with the command: “Look at _that_!” – But what would a language-game look like with the command “See that”? There are various possibilities. E.g., “Imagine …!” or “See this drawing as …!”

### <span class="fac">[265\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> ‘You said “It will stop soon”—were you thinking of the noise or of your pain?’ If he now replies “I was thinking of the piano tuner,” is he stating that this connection existed, or is he suggesting it with these words? – Can I not say _both_? If what he said was true, wasn’t that connection there? And isn’t he nevertheless suggesting one that didn’t exist?

### <span class="fac">[265\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/265.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">3</span> It is therefore often quite irrelevant whether one says “I meant” or “I mean.” It means roughly: The sentence belongs in _this_ context. And it _may_ be that it is only placed in the context through the words “I mean ….”

### <span class="fac">[266\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">1</span> There is a command: “Look at that!” Is there also the command: “See that!”? But one can say: “Imagine that!” & also “See this figure as a cube!”

### <span class="fac">[266\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/266.webp)</span>

<span class="series-number">2</span> I point with my hand & say “Come here!”. A asks “Did you mean me?”. I say “No; B.” What was going on when I meant B (since my pointing left it in doubt whom I meant)? – I said these words, made this hand gesture; did anything else have to happen for the language-game to proceed? But didn’t I already know whom I meant while pointing? Did I know? I would have pointed at B, for example, even if A hadn’t been standing nearby. [The meaning develops.]

### <span class="fac">[266\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/266.webp),[267\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/267.webp)</span>

But could I not also reconstruct from the circumstances whom I meant? I had only looked at B; I needed him for a certain purpose; etc.

### <span class="fac">[267\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/267.webp)</span>

“Of course I meant B; – I didn’t even think of A!” “I wanted B to come to me so that…” All of this points to a larger context.

### <span class="fac">[267\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/267.webp)</span>

So I would like to say _two things_: That I mean B lies in the circumstances & my thoughts – & on the other hand: it lies in what I say & do later.

### <span class="fac">[267\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/267.webp)</span>

One could therefore ask: If I explained later “I meant B,” could that not contradict my earlier thoughts, etc.?

### <span class="fac">[267\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/267.webp)</span>

The circumstances and my thoughts led, for example, to B.

### <span class="fac">[267\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/267.webp),[268\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/268.webp)</span>

But isn’t the question itself somewhat misleading: “What happens there when I mean A?” Isn’t the _point_ rather: _What do I want to communicate_ when I say “I meant B”? Or also: What is the purpose of this sentence?

### <span class="fac">[268\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/268.webp)</span>

Do I, for example, want to communicate to the person to whom I am saying this, the circumstances that led me to my sentence?

### <span class="fac">[268\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/268.webp)</span>

Is “I meant you” similar to “I made everything ready for the journey,” namely a general kind of description that one can think of in detail?

### <span class="fac">[268\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/268.webp)</span>

“I hoped all day that he would come” is a general description.

### <span class="fac">[268\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/268.webp)</span>

“I think of you every day” is also of this kind.

### <span class="fac">[268\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/268.webp)</span>

“With these words I meant _him_”. – What is the other person supposed to conclude from this? Perhaps that _he_ was in my thoughts.

### <span class="fac">[268\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/268.webp),[269\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/269.webp)</span>

“We were talking about _you_ back then.” Here one could continue: “I said …”.

### <span class="fac">[269\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/269.webp)</span>

“I thought of him back then.” This, too, could be elaborated further.

### <span class="fac">[269\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/269.webp)</span>

“With this remark, I meant _him_.” That is different. Suppose someone replied to me: “I had a good understanding of you.” When he read my remark, it immediately occurred to him whom it referred to. That is what I wanted to achieve; not that he might otherwise deduce anything else about my inner workings from it.

### <span class="fac">[269\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/269.webp)</span>

If, with a pointing gesture, I say “This armchair is broken,” then I _mean_ a specific armchair. But the sentence does not (after all) serve to describe to the other person a state of mind of intending.

### <span class="fac">[269\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/269.webp)</span>

If, however, I say “I thought of his death when I said that,” then I want to communicate to the other person what was ‘going on inside me’.

### <span class="fac">[270\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/270.webp)</span>

Isn't it exactly the same with the verb “to understand”? Someone explains the route that I have to take here and there. He asks, “Have you understood me?” I answer, “I have understood it.” – Do I want to communicate to him what was going on inside me during his explanation? – And yet, that could also be communicated.

### <span class="fac">[270\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/270.webp)</span>

Imagine a language-game: I make allusions to different people; the other person is supposed to guess whom I mean.

### <span class="fac">[270\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/270.webp)</span>

If I make an allusion, my glance, my facial expression, may show that it is an allusion. Someone who sees me may say: “He is thinking of a certain person right now.” Or: “He means a certain person right now, although I don’t know who.” I say: “You guessed it; I was really thinking of a certain person.” So he did guess what was going on inside me.

### <span class="fac">[271\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/271.webp)</span>

He could also say: “Can you guess who I was thinking of?” And if I then say, “I think you were thinking of N…,” then one can of course call that guessing an inner process; but on the other hand, the emphasis is on something quite different.

### <span class="fac">[271\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/271.webp)</span>

I could ask two questions:

1) “Could what you said not be applied to N?”

2) “Were you not thinking of N?”

### <span class="fac">[271\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/271.webp)</span>

When I say to someone, “I mean…,” am I not telling them _that I mean_ it? I _am saying_ to them: “I mean….”

### <span class="fac">[271\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/271.webp),[272\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/272.webp)</span>

But isn’t it strange that the case in which I meant A differs psychologically from the one in which I meant B, & that I should nevertheless not be describing precisely this difference with the words “I meant A, not B”? I mean to say: If that is the case, then it must be _an empirical fact_ that the cases differ psychologically, & it would have to be possible that they _did_ _not_ differ in this way.

### <span class="fac">[272\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/272.webp)</span>

I say, “Come here!” and point in the direction of A. B, who is standing next to him, takes a step toward me. I say, “No; A should come.” Would one now regard that as a communication about my inner experiences? Certainly not. – And could one not still draw conclusions from it about processes that took place in me while uttering the command, “Come here!”?

### <span class="fac">[272\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/272.webp),[273\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/273.webp)</span>

But what kind of processes? Could one not assume that, in my command, I looked at A; that my train of thought led me to him? But perhaps I don’t know B at all; I am only connected to A. Then, whoever was conjecturing about my mental processes could have been completely wrong, and yet would still have understood that I meant A and not B.

### <span class="fac">[273\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/273.webp)</span>

A language-game: One invents names that characterize familiar people in some way. The others are supposed to guess who is meant by the name. The game could also be played in such a way that the person who invents the names writes the invented name on a piece of paper as the proper name of the person. So that there is no doubt whom he meant.

### <span class="fac">[273\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/273.webp)</span>

If I draw N’s face from memory here, then one can say that I _mean_ him with my drawing. But what process that takes place during the drawing could one say is the meaning?

For one would naturally say: when he meant him, he was directing his gaze at him. But how does he do that when he recalls his face?

### <span class="fac">[274\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/274.webp)</span>

I mean, how does he recall him? _How does he recall him?_

### <span class="fac">[274\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/274.webp)</span>

And how does he know that the other person is coming? Think about that!

### <span class="fac">[274\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/274.webp)</span>

“I am anxiously awaiting” – that is, after all, the description of a state of mind.

### <span class="fac">[274\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/274.webp)</span>

But what about language-games; to which does a particular expression belong? If, for example, I say, “I expect an explosion any moment now,” is that the communication of a state of expectation, or merely the communication that I believe an explosion will soon occur?

### <span class="fac">[274\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/274.webp)</span>

One could have different ways of speaking. For example, “I expect p” could mean: I believe that this and that will happen. That is to say, it is my opinion, but it does not further _occupy_ me. “I am in the expectation…” should mean: My mind is occupied with this event and is filled with expectation.

### <span class="fac">[275\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/275.webp)</span>

I say “Please come here!” and wave to him. I meant A and not B. I meant him; there is no doubt about it. Even if I glanced at B, I only meant A. And yet it seems clear, if I don’t want to invent something, that there was no other connection between me (or my words) and him than, for example, my gaze, and so on. This ‘meaning him’ conceived of as a connection is like a myth. And a very _strong_ myth. For whatever connection I think of, none does what I want. No connection that I imagine is sufficient, and as a result, it seems as if the very act of meaning is a specific connection, quite _uncomparable_ to all the other connections. (I believe that Moore thought the concept of ‘good’ was indefinable.)

### <span class="fac">[275\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/275.webp),[276\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/276.webp)</span>

Think again of the case “come here!”, but in terms of continuity and discontinuity. I am standing among a lot of people; suddenly, without any reason, I point at one and say “come here!” It seems as if I cannot _completely_ discontinuously want him to come to me. I must establish a certain connection with him. For example, I like his face. Or: I point and look unambiguously at him.

### <span class="fac">[276\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/276.webp)</span>

When I remember, I meant so and so, must I not remember certain circumstances that bear this out?

### <span class="fac">[276\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/276.webp)</span>

“Are you sure you meant A?” – “Of course, I was speaking to A.” Or: “Of course; A has always helped me on such occasions.” Or: “Yes, I meant A. I had never spoken to him before and wanted to get him involved.” Or: “He smiled at me, and on impulse, I waved to him.” Or: “I was in doubt whether to call A or B; finally, I turned to A.”

### <span class="fac">[276\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/276.webp),[277\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/277.webp)</span>

I am talking to someone. We are talking about the fact that I want to demonstrate something to him. Suddenly, without looking at him, I say, “Come here.” I meant him, and he understood. On the other hand: I am in a room with a lot of people. I look at the floor in front of me and say “come here” – and in doing so, I mean N who is somewhere in the room. How do I do that? – If I told someone that I had done that, he would not quite understand it and would ask whether I thought I could, by saying those words and by thinking of N, somehow attract him, conjure him to me. I could also say a curse under my breath and mean a certain person by it. And this could have happened in some way by _thinking_ of him. But should I now say: thinking of him was the same as meaning him?

### <span class="fac">[277\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/277.webp),[278\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/278.webp)</span>

In some spiritualist practices, it is essential that one _thinks_ of a person. And we have here the impression that thinking of him is like spearing him with my thoughts. Or it is as if I were constantly probing him with my thoughts. Because they always stray away from him a little.

### <span class="fac">[278\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/278.webp)</span>

If you tell me that you cursed and meant N, it will be completely irrelevant to me whether you looked at his picture, whether you imagined him, said his name, etc. The inferences from the fact that interest me have nothing to do with that. On the other hand, it could be that someone explains to me that his curse is only effective if he clearly imagines the person, or says his name out loud. And here one could perhaps say “It depends on _how_ the curser _means_ his victim.” But that is not the usual use of “to mean.” Usually, the question “_How_ did you mean him?” does not make sense.

### <span class="fac">[278\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/278.webp),[279\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/279.webp)</span>

If someone tells me “I cursed N back then,” I don’t ask “_How_ did you curse him?” and in any case, it would not mean “How did you refer to him while cursing?”

### <span class="fac">[279\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/279.webp)</span>

One does not, of course, ask: “Are you sure that you cursed _him_, that the connection with _him_ was established?”

### <span class="fac">[279\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/279.webp)</span>

So, is this connection therefore very easy to establish, so that one can be so sure of it?! Can one know that it won't go wrong? – Well, can it happen that I want to write to one person & actually write to the other? & how does that happen?

### <span class="fac">[279\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/279.webp),[280\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/280.webp)</span>

If I have two friends, both named Fritz, and I write a letter to one of them; what is it that prevents me from writing it to the other? The content? But that could fit both. I haven’t written the address yet. Well, the connection might lie in the background. But if someone asks me, “Who are you writing to?” and I answer, “To Fritz N in …”—did I deduce this from the background? Don’t I give the answer almost the same way I say, “I have a toothache”?—Could I be in doubt about which of the two I’m writing to? – _It_ would, of course, be possible that I’m writing a letter and will only decide later which of the two I’ll send it to. And in that case, I could answer the question “Who are you writing to?” with “I don’t know yet.” But I could also answer: “No one for now—I’ll send the letter to …, or to ….”

### <span class="fac">[280\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/280.webp),[281\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/281.webp)</span>

But wouldn't this case also be conceivable: I write a letter without an address; someone asks me “Who are you writing to?”. I answer: “I don't know – I just knew it, but now I've forgotten.” Later I say: “Now I know it, I wrote to N!” – This would be like when one sometimes says: “What did I want to look for in this drawer? – Oh yes, that…!” And when this occurs to us, we remember the connection of our action with the past. But it could also be the case: I open a drawer & rummage through it, suddenly I seem to come to my senses & ask myself “Why am I rummaging in this drawer?”; and then the answer comes: “I want to see the photograph of…”. “I _want_”, not “I _wanted_”. The opening of the drawer etc. happened, as it were, automatically & received _later_ an interpretation.

### <span class="fac">[281\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/281.webp)</span>

And wouldn't it be possible that I am mistaken in my answer – “I am writing to N”? Not as if my memory is deceiving me – about the beginning of the letter. But if I look at the address, I might say: “No! I am writing to M; but it strangely seemed to me at this moment as if I were writing to N.”

### <span class="fac">[281\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/281.webp),[282\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/282.webp)</span>

“You wanted to wave to someone just now; who did you mean?” – The verbal reaction to this is “I meant N”. Now you can remember the process ‘more’ clearly. You then say something like: “I wanted to tell him…, because…”. Or: “I was thinking that… & I wanted to tell him that…”.

### <span class="fac">[282\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/282.webp)</span>

Now one would like to ask: “Could someone who was able to see into your inner self see there that you _wanted_ to say _that_?”. Suppose I had written down my intention on a piece of paper, then another person could have read my intention there; & can I imagine that he could have found out my intention in some other way, _more_ reliably than in this way? Certainly not.

### <span class="fac">[282\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/282.webp)</span>

“I meant him at that time.” When & for how long did you mean him?

### <span class="fac">[282\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/282.webp)</span>

I remember meaning him. Do I remember a process or a state? When did it begin, how did it proceed, etc.?

### <span class="fac">[282\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/282.webp),[283\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/283.webp)</span>

What do I believe when I believe in a soul in a person? What do I believe when I believe that this substance contains two rings of C-atoms? In both cases, an image is in the foreground, but the sense is (far) in the background; i.e., the application of the image is not easily overlooked.

### <span class="fac">[283\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/283.webp),[284\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/284.webp)</span>

We hear that primitive tribes believe they are descended from an animal (e.g., a snake). We ask ourselves: How can they believe that? – We should ask: “How do they believe it?” They say, for example, words that we translate into the English sentence “We are descended from …”. – “But that’s not all!” one might say; after all, they have the most diverse customs and laws, all of which are based on this belief (and thus provide a proof that we have _correctly_ translated their words into German)! But why shouldn’t we say: these customs and laws _are_ not _based_ on that belief, but rather they show _to what extent_, in what sense, such a belief exists. One might ask, for example: Do those people ever believe on other occasions that a snake gives birth to a human instead of a snake? Depending on how this question is answered, their belief in their origin takes on a different dimension.

### <span class="fac">[284\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/284.webp),[285\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/285.webp)</span>

Let us imagine a tribe calling itself “the Children of Israel.” Originally, I assume, this did not refer to the descendants of a man named Israel. Rather, “descendants” or “children” meant something like “_the tribe_,” viewed as a phenomenon in time. This would be like calling the development of π “the children of π.” Now suppose, “through a misunderstanding,” one interprets that expression as “the children (descendants) _of_ Israel” and thus speaks of a man named Israel as the progenitor: the question is: in what cases does one have the right to speak of a misunderstanding; and in what cases only of a figurative mode of expression. Prima facie, it must be assumed that there are in reality _all sorts of degrees_ here. And that in certain religions, what was originally figurative language develops into a full-blown distortion. (With the help, for example, of philosophers.)

### <span class="fac">[285\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/285.webp),[286\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/286.webp)</span>

A language-game: guessing thoughts. Playing cards lie on a table. I want the other person to touch one of them. I close my eyes & think of one of these cards; the other person is to guess which one I mean. He then thinks of a card, wishing to guess my opinion, & touches the card. I say: Yes, that was it, or that wasn’t it. A variation of this game would be that I _look_ at a specific card, but in such a way that the other person cannot see the direction of my gaze & must now guess that card. It is important that this is a variation of the first game.

In the first game, one could also ask me: _What_ card are you thinking of? (And this could be important because it might turn out that, depending on the manner, the guessing would sometimes be more reliable and sometimes less.)

### <span class="fac">[286\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/286.webp)</span>

“I wanted _N_ to come to me”—here there is a question: “Why did you want that?” That is: “How did you come up with that thought?” I want to learn about the context of the wish here. But that is a poor way of putting it. I didn’t want to learn about the context of the wish, but at most the context of the expression of the wish; and, one could say, the _course_ of the wish.

### <span class="fac">[286\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/286.webp)</span>

But did the wish have to take such a course? Couldn’t I suddenly and for no reason wish that N might come to me? And yet that did happen at some point! It had a beginning, an end, etc. So a process must have taken place after all, _similar_ to that of uttering the wish.

### <span class="fac">[286\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/286.webp),[287\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/287.webp)</span>

When I say “I saw an armchair in this room,” I can usually recall the specific visual image only very casually, & in most cases it has no meaning at all. The way the sentence is used ignores this particular detail. Is this also the case when I say “I meant him”: Does this sentence similarly ignore the particulars of the event? So that one could say: “I meant N; but I no longer know (exactly) _how_ it happened.” – What is it that I _cannot_ remember here?

### <span class="fac">[287\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/287.webp),[288\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/288.webp)</span>

In a situation only slightly different, instead of silently waving with his finger, he could have said to someone, “Tell N to come to me.” Now one might say that the words “I wanted N to come to me” describe the state of my mind at the time, and one might also say that they do not.

### <span class="fac">[288\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/288.webp)</span>

What criterion is there for the fact that you really wanted it, or meant it, at the time? Well, another person could recognize it from your behavior and the circumstances; but you do not recognize it from that.

### <span class="fac">[288\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/288.webp)</span>

When I say “I meant _him_,” I may well have an image in mind, for example of how I looked at him, etc., but the image is merely like the illustration in a story. From it alone, one could usually deduce nothing at all; only when one knows the story does one know what the image is meant to convey.

### <span class="fac">[288\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/288.webp)</span>

“But whoever says ‘I meant _him_’ is surely _remembering_ something! Something from the past comes back to their mind!”

### <span class="fac">[288\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/288.webp),[289\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/289.webp)</span>

But do we recognize an experience as a memory? I mean: When we have an experience, do we see an image before us, or something similar, and do we then recognize this image by certain features as that of a past event? Like when one sees a photograph that has yellowed and concludes from this that it was taken a long time ago? But here we need _experience_ to teach us that the color of the paper indicates age.

### <span class="fac">[289\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/289.webp)</span>

Someone makes a move to speak. I don’t let him get a word in. Then I ask, “What did you want to say?”—Now he says something. —What does it matter to me _what_ was going on inside him back then? What does it matter to me how he translates that desire into speech? —But what about him himself?

### <span class="fac">[289\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/289.webp)</span>

Do these two questions have a similar grammar: “What did you want to say?” and “What did you say?”—One could also put it this way: Does remembering an intention mean remembering a process that is certainly not the intended action, but something else of a similar kind?

### <span class="fac">[290\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/290.webp)</span>

Imagine I see a situation in a book, but I don’t know whether it is a situation from the main story, or a memory of the hero, or a dream, or a wish, or a fear of one of the characters.

### <span class="fac">[290\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-116/290.webp)</span>

“I remember that I wanted to stay with him longer at the time.” What image of this wish comes before my mind? None at all. What I see in my memory does not allow any conclusion about my feelings. And yet I remember quite clearly that they were present.
